# PROJECT STATE

## DEV — WAVE 2: VIBE-CODED PURGE (CHEAP PARTS) + LANDING FOOTER — 2026-09-01

Built per Cato's Wave 2 scope above (item 7's cheap parts + item 2's footer gap). Explicitly did not touch the brand gradient and did not do a broader redesign, per Cato's constraint. Branch `claude/futureos-student-app-3ewdz6`, this worktree.

**1) Emoji-in-headings — removed, judgment applied per-site, not a blind strip.**
- `StudentHome.tsx`/`BusinessHome.tsx`: removed the trailing 👋 from the `<h1>` greeting (the exact "Welcome...! 🚀"-style tell the CEO named).
- `missions/page.tsx`: `CATEGORY_TITLES` (`<h2>` section titles) had emoji prefixes (`📚 School Missions`, etc.) — removed; the category icon is already shown per-item in `MissionListItem`, so the heading emoji was redundant, not just decorative.
- `weekly-review/page.tsx`: the 4 student card-section titles (`📚 School`, `🧠 Skills`, `🚀 Future`, `🔥 Consistency`) were raw emoji glyphs while the sibling business "Plan" section already used a real `lucide-react` `Target` icon — a real inconsistency, not just an emoji tell. Fixed by swapping all 4 to matching lucide icons (`BookOpen`, `Brain`, `Rocket`, `Flame`) at the same size/color as the existing `Target` icon, making both tracks consistent instead of just deleting the icon.
- **Left untouched, deliberately:** every emoji used as the app's de facto icon system (subject icons, career icons, achievement badges, mission category tags, mastery-status dots, interest picker, the streak 🔥 in `StatTile`/`StudentHome`/`BusinessHome`/`BrandPanel`). Grepped the full remaining emoji list after the pass and confirmed every remaining instance is a functional icon substitute, not heading/title copy.

**2) Em-dashes in user-facing copy — rewritten sentence-by-sentence, not find-replaced.**
Fixed em-dashes in every genuine JSX text node / user-visible string I could find across: landing page (`page.tsx`), `BrandPanel.tsx` (all 4 auth/onboarding variants' hero quotes + the "82% match" stat), signup/login/choose-plan/auth-callback error and helper copy, both onboarding flows (`StudentOnboarding.tsx`/`BusinessOnboarding.tsx`), the upgrade/pricing page, `PaywallGate.tsx`, homework-help quick-action prompts (both the current and legacy subject-scoped versions — these become the literal text sent to the AI Coach, so worth fixing even though they're "prompt" text), quiz/exam pages, weak-topics, flashcard review results, missions detail page, profile skill list, root `layout.tsx` `<title>` metadata and `branding.ts`'s meta `description`, and 3 catalog content strings (`careers.ts`'s `whyItMatches`/`educationRoutes`, `roadmap.ts` level 2 description, `missions.ts` a mission description) that render as real in-app copy. ~60 individual rewrites, each read and restructured on its own (periods, commas, colons, or the app's existing `·` separator convention for short stat-style labels — not a mechanical dash-to-comma swap).
- **What I didn't get to, and why:** ~77 remaining em-dash instances live in `src/app/api/**/route.ts` — the large majority are Claude system-prompt instruction text (never rendered to a user, sent only to the AI) and a smaller number of thrown `NextResponse.json({ error: ... })` strings that are mostly caught and replaced with generic UI copy rather than rendered verbatim. Per the brief's own prioritization guidance (get the highest-visibility copy first, it's fine to not do a full sweep of buried backend strings), I left these. Also left all remaining em-dashes that are inside `/**...*/` or `//` code comments (dozens, checked file-by-file, confirmed not code that ends up on screen) and the one `"—"` glyph in `school/progress/page.tsx` used as a legitimate "no data yet" placeholder value (not a sentence construction), which is a different, pre-existing UI convention, not the copy issue this pass targets.

**3) Page-load fade — judgment call: reduced on the 5 highest-traffic screens, kept everywhere else.**
`animate-fade-in` (`tailwind.config.ts`: plain `opacity 0→1`, 0.2s ease-out, no translate/scale, no stagger) is genuinely subtle in isolation — I don't think a single instance of it is a "vibe-coded" tell on its own, and said so rather than reflexively stripping it everywhere. But tracing where it's actually mounted matters more than the animation itself: Next.js App Router unmounts/remounts a route's page component on every navigation between different routes (it doesn't persist across route changes the way a shared layout does), so the 5 main bottom-nav/sidebar tab destinations (`StudentHome`/`BusinessHome`, `school/layout.tsx` which wraps the whole School/Plan tab, `StudentFutureHome`/`BusinessGrowHome`, `coach/page.tsx`, `profile/page.tsx`) were re-triggering this fade on **every single tab tap, for the entire lifetime of a session** — dozens of times a day, not once per page load. That repetition, not any individual fade, is what plausibly reads as templated. Removed `animate-fade-in` from those 5 files only. Left it everywhere else it was already used (deeper/secondary routes — `missions`, `missions/[id]`, `weekly-review`, `upgrade`, `deadlines`, `homework-help`, `session`, `plan/new`, `plan/[planId]`, `future/[slug]` — plus `Modal.tsx`/`Tooltip.tsx` micro-interactions and onboarding, all of which are visited far less often per session and where a one-time "new context has loaded" fade is a reasonable, not-templated-feeling transition).

**4) Landing page footer — added.** `src/app/page.tsx` restructured from a single flex-row (`hero column` + `BrandPanel`) into a flex-col wrapping [the same flex-row, unchanged, now inside its own wrapper div] + a new full-width `<footer>` below it, so the footer spans under both the hero column and the desktop `BrandPanel` rather than being squeezed into the left column. Footer: `LogoMark` + brand name, 3 links (Privacy/Terms/FAQ), copyright line with `new Date().getFullYear()`. Reused only existing typography/spacing/color tokens (`text-sm`/`text-xs`, `text-muted-foreground`, `border-border`) — no new design-system additions, brand gradient untouched.
- **Privacy/Terms/FAQ:** scaffolded real stub pages (`src/app/privacy/page.tsx`, `/terms`, `/faq`) via one new shared shell (`src/components/shared/StaticContentPage.tsx`) rather than shipping dead `href="/privacy"` links that 404 today. Privacy/Terms stubs are deliberately non-committal placeholder copy ("full policy is being finalized and will be published here") — I did not author actual legal claims or terms, per Cato's own flag that legal content needs CEO review, not an agent's invention. FAQ content is factual product description I could verify directly against the shipped code (free-tier vs. Plus gating, track-lock-at-onboarding) and is safe to publish as-is; still worth a CEO/Product glance before calling FAQ "final," but it isn't legal content.

**Verification:**
- `npx tsc --noEmit`: clean (exit 0), run directly.
- `npx next build`: clean (exit 0), all 46 routes including the 3 new stub routes (`/privacy`, `/terms`, `/faq` at 181 B each).
- Playwright (Chromium at `/opt/pw-browsers/chromium-1194`, real compiled CSS from this repo, `next start` against the production build): landing page screenshotted at 390px and 1280px, dark and light (theme resolved via `localStorage` + the app's own `themeInitScript`, not guessed) — footer renders full-width, correctly under `BrandPanel` on desktop, no overflow/wrap/collision with the hero content in any of the 4 combinations. `/privacy` (390px) and `/faq` (1280px) also screenshotted and render cleanly.
- Did **not** get a live-authenticated screenshot of the emoji/icon-swap or fade-removal changes (`StudentHome`, `weekly-review`, etc.) — this sandbox has the same Supabase-egress restriction noted in the prior full-app QA pass, so there's no way to reach a real logged-in session here. Confirmed instead via `curl` that `/app`, `/app/weekly-review`, `/app/missions` all return 200 (no server crash) against the production build, plus the clean `tsc`/`build`, plus the changes themselves are simple (icon swap, class removal, text edits) with no new logic. Flagging this gap explicitly rather than claiming a visual check that didn't happen — QA has live-DB access in a prior pass and should do the authenticated visual pass this session couldn't.

Files touched: `src/app/page.tsx`, `src/app/privacy/page.tsx` (new), `src/app/terms/page.tsx` (new), `src/app/faq/page.tsx` (new), `src/components/shared/StaticContentPage.tsx` (new), `src/components/shared/BrandPanel.tsx`, `src/components/shared/PaywallGate.tsx`, `src/lib/branding.ts`, `src/lib/catalog/careers.ts`, `src/lib/catalog/missions.ts`, `src/lib/catalog/roadmap.ts`, `src/app/layout.tsx`, `src/app/login/page.tsx`, `src/app/signup/page.tsx`, `src/app/choose-plan/page.tsx`, `src/app/auth/callback/page.tsx`, `src/app/onboarding/StudentOnboarding.tsx`, `src/app/onboarding/BusinessOnboarding.tsx`, `src/app/app/StudentHome.tsx`, `src/app/app/BusinessHome.tsx`, `src/app/app/coach/page.tsx`, `src/app/app/profile/page.tsx`, `src/app/app/upgrade/page.tsx`, `src/app/app/weekly-review/page.tsx`, `src/app/app/missions/page.tsx`, `src/app/app/missions/[id]/page.tsx`, `src/app/app/school/layout.tsx`, `src/app/app/future/StudentFutureHome.tsx`, `src/app/app/future/BusinessGrowHome.tsx`, and the school/study-flow pages listed in the em-dash section above.

BLOCKERS: None. NEXT: QA review, including the authenticated-screen visual pass this session couldn't do.

## CATO — TRIAGE + PHASED PLAN FOR CEO'S 17-ITEM BUG/FEATURE REPORT — 2026-09-01

CEO sent a large, unstructured 17-item report (bugs + features + a full launch-readiness checklist). This section is Cato's investigation of what's actually true in the code right now for each item, followed by a phased execution plan. Nothing in this section has been built yet — this is planning only, per instruction. **Housekeeping note:** `PROJECT_STATE.md` was several commits stale at the tail before this pass — the last few entries in the file (ending at QA's round-3 signup-OTP review) predate `aa0c574`/`43261a9`/`5b7758f`/`99a076b` (cross-account profile-leak fix, email-confirmation-link routed through `/auth/callback`, **business track marked "coming soon" on `/choose-plan`**, and **Google Sign-In removed entirely**). All of those are real, merged, on `HEAD` (`99a076b`) — verified via `git log`, not assumed. The business-track pause is relevant to several items below and is called out where it matters.

### Item-by-item findings

**1) "Fake info when first logged in" — REAL, and worse than it sounds.** `src/lib/onboarding/completeOnboarding.ts` inserts a full fabricated `timetable_entries`/`homework`/`exams`/`study_sessions` set for every new student, straight into the real per-user tables, at signup. Two paths: `src/lib/onboarding/researchSchool.ts` tries an AI web-search call to *guess* a plausible curriculum/timetable for the student's actual school/year/subjects (`src/app/api/onboarding/research-school/route.ts`), and if that fails/times out (55s bound, "routinely takes 25-40s+" per its own code comment — fails often) it silently falls back to `src/lib/seed/demoData.ts`'s `buildDemoData()`, which is fully generic (rotates through the student's chosen subjects with template assignment titles like "Lab Report", "Algebra Practice Set"). **Neither path is the student's real homework/exams/timetable.** Confirmed via grep: there is **no delete UI anywhere** for `homework`, `exams`, or `timetable_entries` (only `study_materials`/`study_plans`/`study_subjects` have delete). A student can add real items on top (Deadlines-initiative forms) and mark homework complete, but the fabricated exams and timetable are **permanent and unremovable** — this is why it looks fake *every single login*, forever, exactly as the CEO described. This is a real, first-priority product-integrity bug, not a misread empty state.

**2) "No landing page" — STALE as literally stated, but with a real, narrower gap underneath.** `src/app/page.tsx` (post round-3 UI redesign) is a genuine, well-composed landing screen: `LogoMark`, headline, subcopy, primary CTA, a real proof strip, and a desktop `BrandPanel` two-column layout — this is not the empty centered-column complaint round 3 was built to fix. But it is a **single hero screen, not a marketing page**: no nav bar, no footer, no pricing/features/FAQ section, and critically **no links to Privacy/Terms/FAQ anywhere** (confirmed: those pages don't exist at all, see item 6). So "no landing page" is inaccurate; "the landing page has no footer, no FAQ, no legal links, and nothing to link to even if it did" is accurate.

**3) Ownership checks — SOLID, verified directly, not just carried forward from old QA notes.** Read all 16 `src/app/api/*/route.ts` files: every single one calls `requireUser(req)` first (JWT-verified via `supabase.auth.getUser()`, never a client-supplied id) and every DB read/write is scoped by `.eq("user_id", user.id)` / `.eq("id", user.id)`, with RLS as an independent backstop (already impersonation-tested live against production on 2026-08-27, unchanged schema since). The two service-role writes that exist (`create-subscription`, the Stripe webhook) are both scoped to `user.id` from a verified session, or authenticated via Stripe's own signature (webhook has no user session by design). No bypasses found.

**4) Parameterized queries — SOLID.** Grepped the entire codebase for raw SQL, string-concatenated queries, and `.rpc()` calls: **zero matches.** Every single database call goes through the Supabase JS client's query builder (`.select()`/`.insert()`/`.eq()`/etc.), which is inherently parameterized. SQL injection is structurally not possible via this pattern anywhere in the app.

**5) Key separation — SOLID.** `src/lib/supabase/client.ts` (the only browser-facing Supabase client) uses only `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_ANON_KEY`. `SUPABASE_SERVICE_ROLE_KEY` is referenced in exactly 3 files, all server-only (`src/lib/supabase/server.ts`, and the two routes above), none of them client components (grepped for `"use client"` + the service-role key together — zero hits). No leak found.

**Items 3-5 verdict: no code changes needed.** This is a real, clean finding — say so plainly rather than inventing work.

**6) The ~35-item launch checklist — gone through individually, not padded:**
- **Already done:** onboarding (exists, if seeding fake data per item 1), sign up/log in (exists, hardened through 4 real QA rounds), email verification (OTP + link-via-`/auth/callback`, both shipped), data persistence (Supabase Postgres, verified), payment flow (Stripe subscription + webhook + portal, code-solid), user permissions/ownership (see items 3-5), loading states (`LoadingScreen` used at 19+ sites), meta titles/descriptions (root `layout.tsx` has real `title`/`description` via `branding`), favicon (Next `icon.png` convention + `apple-touch-icon` in `public/icons`), mobile responsiveness (extensively hardened — 4+ real overflow bugs found and fixed across QA history), accessibility — partial (`:focus-visible` ring shipped app-wide in round 1; the app's one `<img>`-equivalent, `LogoMark`, correctly uses `alt=""` as decorative; but only 11 `aria-label` uses total and no accessibility audit has ever been run).
- **Partially done / real gaps found by code, not assumed:**
  - **Empty states:** genuinely built (`EmptyState` component, ~20 adopted sites per prior QA).
  - **Error states / network states: real, systemic gap.** `useTableRows` (the hook every list screen reads through) exposes `{data, loading, error}` — but grepped every consuming page in `src/app`: **zero of them ever destructure or render `error`.** A genuine fetch failure (RLS misconfig, outage, dropped connection) renders identically to "you have nothing" — the user can never tell the difference. This is worth fixing; it's small (the plumbing already exists, it's just never read).
  - **Responsiveness:** "all devices" is doing a lot of work — only 390px/1280px have real Playwright coverage across QA history; no tablet-width (768-1024px) verification has ever been done.
- **Not done / genuine gaps:**
  - **Password reset:** **does not exist at all.** No `resetPasswordForEmail` call, no `/reset-password` route, no "Forgot password?" link on `/login` (grepped, confirmed absent). A user who forgets their password has zero self-service recovery. This is a severe, real gap for a production app with real users.
  - **Account deletion:** does not exist anywhere (grepped, confirmed).
  - Custom 404 (`not-found.tsx`) and error boundary (`error.tsx`): neither exists — the app falls through to Next's bare defaults.
  - Privacy policy, Terms page, FAQ: none exist as pages or content.
  - robots.txt, sitemap.xml: neither exists (checked both the literal-file and Next's `robots.ts`/`sitemap.ts` app-router conventions — zero).
  - OG tags / social share preview, canonical tags: `layout.tsx`'s `metadata` object has no `openGraph`/`twitter`/`alternates.canonical` — sharing a link to Alxioum today renders with no preview card.
  - Cookie consent banner: does not exist.
  - Google Analytics / any analytics: **zero analytics of any kind** in the codebase — no `gtag`, no GA snippet, nothing.
  - Crash reporting: none (no Sentry/Bugsnag/equivalent in `package.json` or code).
- **Out of scope right now, flagged not padded:** beta testers is a CEO/go-to-market action (recruit real people), not a code task — nothing to build. "Test forms" / "check broken links" / "optimize performance" are audit *activities*, not fixed-scope deliverables — sized separately below, not bundled into a checkbox.
- **Real tension worth flagging, not silently complying with:** the CEO's own item 6 says "load GA before the cookie banner" — doing that literally means GA can set tracking cookies before the user has consented, which is the specific thing cookie-consent banners exist to prevent (and this is a student app that may have minors as users — already flagged as a live privacy concern in the Deadlines-initiative history when the achievements-leaderboard idea was rejected for the same reason). Recommend GA loads in a consent-gated/anonymized mode instead, not literally "before" consent — flagging for a CEO call, not silently building the literal ask.

**7) "Vibe-coded" tells — checked each one named, precisely, against the current shipped code:**
- **Purple-to-blue/cyan gradient — REAL, and it's the core brand identity, not a stray bug.** `globals.css`: `--accent: 252 92% 68%` (indigo/purple) → `--accent-end: 186 84% 58%` (cyan). Used in `bg-gradient-brand`/`text-gradient-brand` at **37 sites** across the app (buttons, headings, glows). This is a real, if very large-scope, change — it's not a one-line fix, it's the brand.
- **Gradient hero text — REAL.** Landing page headline: `<span className="text-gradient-brand">grades.</span>` — one word gradiented, exactly the pattern round 3's own diagnosis flagged as an "AI slop" tell (and round 3 explicitly chose not to touch this).
- **Emojis in headings — REAL, confirmed by direct string search, not a hunch.** `StudentHome.tsx`/`BusinessHome.tsx` greeting headings end in `👋`; `weekly-review/page.tsx` and `missions/page.tsx` section headings are prefixed `📚 School`, `🧠 Skills`, `🚀 Future`, `🔥 Consistency`, `💼 Business Missions`, etc. Beyond headings specifically, emoji is used as the app's de facto icon system in many more places (subject icons, interest tags, streak flame) — a broader pattern than just headings.
- **Inter font everywhere — REAL, confirmed.** `layout.tsx` loads `Inter` from `next/font/google` as the only font, app-wide, no exceptions.
- **Colored-border cards — REAL but contained, not indiscriminate.** The base `Card` component (`src/components/ui/Card.tsx`) has no colored border by default. The colored-border pattern (`border-accent/20`, `border-accent/30`, `border-success/40`) is opt-in and concentrated on ~10-12 specific "hero"/featured cards (Home dashboards, the upgrade/pricing page) — real, worth revisiting, but not applied to every card in the app the way the complaint might imply.
- **Fade-in-on-scroll — NOT what's actually there; a narrower, milder pattern is.** Grepped for `whileInView`/`IntersectionObserver` (the actual scroll-triggered-reveal mechanism): **zero matches.** What exists instead is `animate-fade-in`, a plain page-mount transition applied to ~25 page-wrapper `<div>`s (the whole screen fades in once on load, not per-element as you scroll). This is a real, if much milder, instance of "everything fades" — correcting the record before scoping it: don't build a fix for scroll-triggered animation that doesn't exist; the actual, smaller fix is reconsidering the blanket page-load fade. (The literal `FadeIn.tsx` component some agents flagged before is dead/unused code, unrelated to this pattern — confirmed unimported again.)
- **Buttons that fade on hover — MOSTLY NOT TRUE.** The shared `Button` component (used for essentially every real button in the app) uses `hover:brightness-110` (primary/mission) or `hover:bg-*` (secondary/ghost/outline) — only the rarely-used `danger` variant uses `hover:opacity-90`. Grepped `hover:opacity` app-wide: 3 total matches, 2 of which are deliberate glow-blur hover effects (nav icons), not button fades. This specific complaint does not match current reality.
- **Em dashes everywhere in copy — REAL and pervasive.** Sampled and confirmed genuinely in user-facing strings (not just code comments): error messages, empty-state subtitles, placeholder text, AI-tutor prompt framing, and the landing page's own hero subcopy all use em-dash constructions — dozens of real instances across the app. Notably, this project's own `PROJECT_STATE.md` history (written by prior agent rounds) is also saturated with em dashes, which is a good tell that this is a systemic "AI voice" pattern worth a deliberate copy pass, not a one-off.

**8) "How old are you" — REAL gap, but it's a decision, not just a form field.** No age/birthdate field exists anywhere (only "year group," a school-grade proxy, which doesn't map cleanly to age across countries/curricula). Before building: **does this gate anything** (e.g. under-13 handling, parental consent, COPPA-style restriction) or is it purely informational/analytics? This app has already flagged minors-as-users as a live privacy consideration once before (the rejected achievements-leaderboard idea, Deadlines-initiative history). If it's meant to gate something, that's a real compliance decision, not a UI task — flagging for a CEO call before Wave 4 touches this.

**9) Tutorial for new users — REAL, fully missing.** Grepped for any tour/walkthrough/coachmark library or pattern: zero. After the onboarding Q&A, a user lands straight in `/app` with no guided introduction to the 5 tabs or any feature.

**10) "Add to home screen" mobile instructions — REAL gap, but the hard infra is already done.** Full PWA plumbing already exists and works: `public/manifest.json` (real icons, `display: standalone`), `public/sw.js` (a real, deliberately network-first service worker), `<meta appleWebApp>` in `layout.tsx`. What's missing is purely the **in-app UI** teaching a mobile visitor how to actually install it — genuinely zero UI for this anywhere (grepped). This is a small, well-scoped addition on top of infra that already works, not a build-from-zero.

**11) Roadmap/career page unclear next step — REAL, and worse than "unclear": it's structurally inert.** `roadmap_progress` (the table backing the Future-tab roadmap timeline) is written to **exactly once in the entire codebase** — at onboarding (`completeOnboarding.ts`, level 1 auto-completed, level 2 auto-unlocked) — and never updated again anywhere. Every user's roadmap is permanently frozen after signup; there is no action anywhere in the app that ever advances a level. The 6 roadmap levels (`src/lib/catalog/roadmap.ts`) are static descriptive text ("Build your first project") with no linked action/checklist/CTA. This isn't just unclear copy — the feature has no mechanism to progress at all.

**12) Coach chat naming — HALF ALREADY DONE, verify-before-build worked exactly as intended.** AI auto-naming: **already shipped and working** — `src/app/api/coach/route.ts` fires a dedicated Claude call ("Give a 3-6 word title summarizing this conversation") the first time a thread gets a reply, and writes it to `chat_threads.title`. User-initiated rename: **does not exist** — grepped the whole Coach page for any rename affordance (edit icon, editable title, update call for `title`) — none found, thread titles are read-only in the UI. Build only the missing half.

**13) "Homework tab isn't there" — REAL, precise structural gap, confirmed.** `SchoolSubNav.tsx` has 6 tabs: Home/Subjects/Exams/Flashcards/Quizzes/Progress — **no Homework tab**, while Exams (a very similar due-date-bearing item type) got promoted to its own full sub-nav tab and dedicated `/app/school/exams` page. Homework only exists buried inside `StudentSchoolHome.tsx` (the School "Home" sub-view) with no standalone list page. This is exactly the asymmetry the CEO is describing, not a misunderstanding.

**14) Add homework/lessons/exams + mock-exam-from-past-paper — split findings, size varies a lot by sub-part.**
- Adding homework/exams: **already exists** (built during an earlier reconciliation round, item 1's "no add-exam/homework UI" QA bug, now fixed).
- Adding "the day's lessons" (timetable): **does not exist** — `timetable_entries` is read-only everywhere, only ever written once at onboarding (ties directly back to item 1 — this is the same permanent-fake-data problem for the timetable specifically). Real, and worth bundling with item 1's fix rather than treating separately.
- **Mock-exam-from-past-paper: partially overlaps existing infra, but is a genuinely new capability, not a duplicate.** There is already a real "Exam Mode" (`src/app/app/school/quizzes/exam-mode/page.tsx`) — a timed, AI-generated quiz at exam-level difficulty, tied to a subject. But it generates generically from the subject's curriculum, **not** from an uploaded/pasted past paper's actual content/style, which is specifically what the CEO is asking for. The building blocks exist (the `study_materials` upload pipeline already supports paste-text/PDF/photo upload + AI analysis, see item 15) but the "generate an exam grounded in *this specific* uploaded paper" generation path does not exist and needs a new AI route. Medium-sized, not small, not a full new subsystem.

**15) "Notes" feature (paste notes / book photos → AI organizes them) — ALREADY BUILT, this is exactly the "verify before duplicating" case the brief called out.** `src/app/app/school/subjects/[subjectId]/materials/new/page.tsx` already supports pasted text, typed notes, PDF upload, and **photo upload** (`accept="image/*"`, i.e. book-page photos). `src/app/api/study/analyze-material/route.ts` already runs this through Claude and extracts topics/summaries/key concepts/terms/practice questions, saved to `study_topics` and viewable later. **Do not build parallel infrastructure.** The real, narrow gaps: (a) it's subject-scoped (requires picking/creating a `study_subject` first) rather than a flat, always-available "Notes" entry point, and (b) it's not labeled or discoverable as "Notes" anywhere in the UI/nav — it lives under Subjects → [subject] → Materials. If the CEO's intent is "organized notes" specifically (vs. the current topic/term/quiz-question extraction framing), that's a copy/output-shape tweak on an existing feature, not a new build.

**16) Achievements "fixed so they work" — mechanism is NOT broken; the actual problem is silence, not malfunction.** Traced all 9 achievement definitions to their award call sites: every one has a real, reachable trigger condition (first study session, 10 sessions, first quiz, first mock exam, 20 mastered flashcards, 7-day streak, first career mission, first project, career path chosen), all idempotent via a unique-key upsert, all correctly rendered locked/unlocked (with a lock icon + grayscale) on the Profile page. **What's actually missing: zero celebratory feedback anywhere** — grepped for any unlock toast/modal/notification: none. An achievement is awarded completely silently; the only way to notice is to manually check Profile later, which reads exactly like "achievements don't work" from a user's seat even though the backend is fine. Secondary, smaller findings: `career_path_chosen` is awarded to nearly every user automatically at onboarding (barely an "achievement"), and the business track has zero business-flavored achievements (all track-specific ones are `student`-only) — lower priority given business signups are currently paused.

**17) Notifications system — genuinely does not exist, confirmed, not assumed.** No notification bell/center UI anywhere (grepped). No push infrastructure (`sw.js` has zero `push`/`notificationclick` listeners — it's a static-asset cache only). No general transactional-email capability in app code — `resend` isn't even an npm dependency; Resend today is purely a Supabase Auth SMTP provider configured in the dashboard for auth emails (OTP/confirmation), not something app code can call for arbitrary notifications (exam reminders, etc.) without adding it as a real new integration. **This needs a CEO decision before any build**, not a guess: push (needs a new service — no push infra exists at all today, this project has repeatedly avoided adding new external delivery infra) vs. email (Resend already exists for auth, more feasible to extend, but is a real new integration for arbitrary app-triggered sends) vs. in-app-only (cheapest, matches the precedent the Deadlines initiative already set of deliberately choosing in-app-only over email/push for the same reason).

### Phased execution plan

**Wave 1 — Security/correctness audit + the one severe pre-existing bug (items 1, 3, 4, 5).**
- Items 3, 4, 5: **no action needed** — confirmed solid by direct code read. Close these out, don't invent work.
- Item 1: fix the fake-onboarding-data problem. Two parts: (a) stop treating fabricated/AI-guessed data as real, permanent rows — at minimum, give exams/homework/timetable entries real delete/edit UI (the missing piece that turns "fake and stuck forever" into "fake and correctable"), and/or clearly mark AI-seeded items as suggestions the student can accept/discard rather than silently-real data; (b) fold in item 14's "add the day's lessons" (timetable) gap here since it's the same root cause. Product should spec the exact UX (delete/edit affordance vs. a "starter suggestions, review and confirm" onboarding step) before Dev builds — this is a product decision, not just a CRUD add.
- Also close the small, real gap found in this pass: wire up `error` from `useTableRows` into at least the highest-traffic screens (Home, School, Exams) so a fetch failure is visibly distinct from "you have nothing" — small, mechanical, high-value.

**Wave 2 — Vibe-coded purge (item 7) + landing page real gaps (item 2).**
- Cheap, low-risk, high-value first: emoji-in-headings removal, em-dash copy pass (real and pervasive, per findings above), reconsidering the blanket page-load fade.
- Bigger, real decision needed from the CEO before starting: the purple-to-cyan brand gradient is the core brand identity (37 usages) — changing it is a rebrand-scale decision, not a bug fix. Flagging for explicit CEO sign-off before Product/Dev touch it, given 3 rounds of prior UI work were built around this exact palette.
- Landing page: add a footer with links to Privacy/Terms/FAQ (once those exist, see Wave 3) — small, but blocked on Wave 3 content existing first.

**Wave 3 — Compliance/SEO/content checklist (item 6), split by effort:**
- *Quick, mechanical, no content decisions needed:* `robots.ts`/`sitemap.ts` (Next app-router convention), custom `not-found.tsx` + `error.tsx`, OG/Twitter meta tags + `alternates.canonical` in `layout.tsx`, cookie consent banner (mechanical to add; the *content/legal* question of what it must say is not).
- *Needs real content/decisions the CEO must provide or approve, not something an agent should invent:* Privacy Policy and Terms of Service copy (legal content — an agent should not author binding legal text unreviewed), FAQ copy, and **Google Analytics specifically needs a GA4 property/measurement ID the CEO creates or provides** — this project has no Google Analytics MCP tool to create one automatically, and per the "vibe-coded" findings above, the literal "load GA before the cookie banner" instruction needs a consent-mode adjustment, not literal compliance, given the minors-as-users consideration already on record in this project.
- Password reset and account deletion belong here too, sized as real, non-trivial features (password reset touches the same `AuthProvider`/session surface that took 4 QA rounds to get right for OTP signup — budget accordingly, don't rush it).

**Wave 4 — UX gaps (items 9, 10, 11, 12, 13, 16) — smaller, contained, mostly additive:**
- Item 13 (homework gets its own sub-nav tab + page, matching Exams' precedent) and item 12 (add user-rename UI next to the already-shipped AI auto-naming) are the smallest, most mechanical of this wave.
- Item 10 (home-screen install instructions, desktop-hidden) is small and has zero infra risk (the PWA plumbing already works).
- Item 16: the fix that actually matters here is a lightweight "achievement unlocked" moment (toast/banner), not touching the award logic, which isn't broken.
- Item 11 (roadmap) needs a real product decision on what actually advances a level — right now nothing does, so this is closer to "design a real mechanism" than "fix a bug."
- Item 9 (tutorial) is the most open-ended of this wave — Product should scope it (a short guided-tour overlay vs. a one-time interstitial) before Dev builds.
- Item 8 (age question) is blocked on the CEO decision flagged above (does it gate anything) before it's built.

**Wave 5 — Larger new builds (items 14's mock-exam-from-past-paper, 15, 17) — sized honestly, each needs its own Product spec pass, not scoped in detail here:**
- Item 15 (notes): **smallest of the three, because it's mostly a reframing/promotion of the existing `study_materials` feature**, not new infrastructure — Product should decide whether to just relabel/surface it better or add a genuinely flat (non-subject-scoped) entry point.
- Item 14 (mock exam from an uploaded past paper): medium — reuses the existing upload pipeline and exam-taking UI, but needs a real new AI generation route grounded in the uploaded material's actual content, which doesn't exist today.
- Item 17 (notifications): the largest of the three, and **blocked on a CEO decision** (in-app-only, vs. extending Resend into a general transactional-email capability, vs. push — which has zero existing infra and this project has consistently avoided adding new external delivery infra for good reason). Recommend in-app-only as the default answer unless the CEO specifically wants email/push, following the same reasoning the Deadlines initiative already used for the identical question.

### Flagged for explicit CEO decision before the relevant wave starts (not guessed at)
- **Item 7's brand gradient removal** — this is a rebrand-scale call (Wave 2), not a bug fix; 3 rounds of prior UI work are built around the current palette.
- **Item 8's age question** — purely informational/analytics, or does it gate something (minors handling)? (Wave 4)
- **Item 6's Google Analytics** — needs a real GA4 property/measurement ID from the CEO; no MCP tool can create one. Also needs a call on consent-mode vs. the literal "before the cookie banner" instruction, given the minors-as-users consideration on record. (Wave 3)
- **Item 6's Privacy Policy / Terms / FAQ copy** — legal/policy content an agent should not author unreviewed and ship as binding. (Wave 3)
- **Item 17's notification channel** — in-app (cheap, matches existing precedent) vs. email (real new Resend integration) vs. push (no infra exists, historically avoided). (Wave 5)
- **Item 1's fix shape** — delete/edit UI for fabricated items, vs. a "review and confirm AI-suggested starter data" onboarding step, vs. dropping the AI-seeding approach entirely in favor of a genuinely empty start. Product should spec this before Dev builds, but the CEO should weigh in on the general direction given how central onboarding's "feels alive from screen one" design goal was to begin with (per `demoData.ts`'s own code comment).

### DEV — Wave 1 build (Item 1's delete/edit-UI fix shape + the `useTableRows` error gap) — QA-reviewed, SIGNED OFF

Cato flagged two things in Wave 1: item 1 (seeded exams/homework/timetable are permanently stuck — no delete UI anywhere) and the `useTableRows.error` gap (fetched but never rendered on any page). Built both, no new tables/columns/migrations, no deviation from the existing add-item/confirm-delete conventions already shipped in this codebase.

**Fix A — exams/homework/timetable are now editable/deletable, not permanently stuck:**
- `src/app/app/school/exams/page.tsx`: every exam card gets a delete button (Trash2 icon → `confirm()` → `.delete().eq("id", examId)` → refetch, same pattern as `subjects/[subjectId]/page.tsx`'s `deleteSubject`/`materials/[materialId]/page.tsx`'s `deleteMaterial`) and an edit-date affordance (Pencil icon toggles an inline `<input type="date">` + Save/Cancel, reusing the file's own existing `linkingId`-toggle interaction pattern for "Link a subject"). Editing is scoped to the date only (the field most likely to be wrong on a seeded/AI-guessed exam) — not a full edit modal, per the brief's "don't over-engineer" instruction.
- `src/app/app/school/StudentSchoolHome.tsx`:
  - Homework: added a delete button (Trash2) to every homework card, visible whether pending or completed — same confirm-then-delete pattern.
  - Timetable ("Today's Classes"): added a delete button to every entry, plus a **new add-class form** (day-of-week select defaulting to today, subject text input, start/end `<input type="time">`, optional room) — this UI didn't exist anywhere before (confirmed by Cato's grep: `timetable_entries` was write-once-at-onboarding, read-only everywhere). Mirrors the existing add-homework/add-exam form shape exactly (same `h-11 rounded-full` input styling, same disabled-until-valid submit button). Note: the display only ever shows *today's* classes (pre-existing behavior, unchanged) — an entry added for a different day won't appear until that day arrives; not redesigning "Today's Classes" into a full weekly view, which would be scope creep beyond "make seeded data correctable."
- All three tables (`exams`, `homework`, `timetable_entries`) already carry the `_all_own` RLS policy (`user_id = auth.uid()`) — every insert/delete/update call here is a plain `.insert()`/`.delete()`/`.update()` through the existing browser Supabase client, no `.upsert()` anywhere (checked — these tables aren't `profiles`, so upsert would actually be fine here too, but plain insert/update/delete matches the codebase's existing convention on these specific tables and needs no conflict resolution).
- Business milestones already had due-date display (Deadlines initiative) but no delete UI — **out of scope for this pass**, not requested by Cato's Wave 1 brief (which named exams/homework/timetable specifically) and milestones aren't AI-seeded at onboarding the way exams/homework/timetable are, so they don't have the "permanently stuck fake data" problem this fix targets.

**Fix B — `useTableRows`'s `error` is now rendered on the 6 highest-traffic pages Cato named:**
- `src/app/app/school/StudentSchoolHome.tsx`, `src/app/app/school/BusinessPlanHome.tsx`, `src/app/app/future/BusinessGrowHome.tsx`, `src/app/app/deadlines/page.tsx`, `src/app/app/StudentHome.tsx`, `src/app/app/BusinessHome.tsx` (plus `src/app/app/school/exams/page.tsx` while already in that file for Fix A) all now destructure `error` from their `useTableRows`-backed hooks and render it via the exact pre-existing danger-card pattern already used 6+ times elsewhere in this codebase (`quizzes/page.tsx`, `flashcards/page.tsx`, `quizzes/exam-mode/page.tsx`, etc.): `<Card className="border border-danger/40"><CardContent className="flex items-start gap-2.5 p-4 text-sm text-danger"><TriangleAlert .../><span>...</span></CardContent></Card>`. Not a new UI convention — copied verbatim from the existing pattern.
- Pages reading through multiple `useTableRows`-backed hooks (most of them) combine errors with a `??` chain — first non-null wins, documented inline as intentional (surfacing every possible simultaneous fetch failure at once would be noisy; any single failure is equally worth flagging).
- `StudentHome.tsx`/`BusinessHome.tsx` are the two CSS-Grid dashboards — the error banner is placed as a full-width block **above and outside** the `lg:grid` container (in the same non-grid header wrapper as the greeting), not as a grid child, so it needs no `col-start`/`row-start` and cannot perturb the existing pinned grid positions. Verified by grep: every existing grid item in both files still has its original paired `col-start`/`row-start`, unchanged by this diff.

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean (exit 0). `npx next build` clean (exit 0), all 42 routes compiled including `/app/school/exams` (4.89 kB) and `/app/deadlines` (6.46 kB). Playwright-rendered a real-classNames static harness (Chromium at `/opt/pw-browsers/chromium-1194`, actual compiled CSS copied from this build's own `.next/static/css` output, not approximated styles) at 390px in both dark and light themes covering: the error banner, the exam card's edit/delete UI + inline date editor, the homework delete button, and the new timetable delete button + add-class form — no overflow, clipping, or truncation issues in either theme. Also rendered the `StudentHome` header+banner+grid structure at 390px and 1280px to confirm the error banner's placement outside the grid doesn't shift any of the existing pinned cells (all 6 grid items landed in their original row/column in both viewports).

**Not yet done:** QA review. This is unreviewed Dev output, not signed off.

### QA REVIEW (Wave 1, commit `9e5e356`, reviewed against Cato's Wave 1 brief) — SIGN-OFF GIVEN

Read `git show 9e5e356` in full (8 files, +347/-33) — not sampled. Fast-forward-merged the commit into this worktree (`git merge --ff-only 9e5e356`, clean parent match on `23e388e`), symlinked the sibling repo's `node_modules` (this worktree had none), and ran everything directly rather than trusting Dev's report.

**Ownership/security on the new deletes/updates/inserts (item 2 of the brief):**
- `exams`/`homework`/`timetable_entries` in `supabase/schema.sql`: all three carry `for all to authenticated using (user_id = auth.uid()) with check (user_id = auth.uid())` (`exams_all_own`, `homework_all_own`, `timetable_all_own`). Grepped the whole schema for `grant update`/`grant insert`/`grant delete`/`grant select` — the **only** column-restricted grant in the entire file is on `profiles` (billing columns); none of the three tables this diff touches have any column restriction. So every new `.delete().eq("id", …)` and `.update({...}).eq("id", …)` call (none of them also filter on `user_id`) is safe purely because RLS's `USING` clause backstops it — matches the pre-existing convention already used by `deleteSubject`/`deleteMaterial` elsewhere in this codebase, not a new pattern invented for this diff.
- No `.upsert()` anywhere in the actual diff (grepped the real diff, not just the commit message's claim) — every write is a plain `.insert()`/`.update()`/`.delete()`. None of the three touched tables is `profiles`, so this specific footgun doesn't apply here regardless.
- Confirmed no billing/entitlement columns anywhere near this diff — irrelevant table set for that concern.

**The new add-class/timetable form (item 3):**
- `timetable_entries` NOT NULL columns per `schema.sql`: `user_id`, `day_of_week` (int, `check between 0 and 6`), `start_time`, `end_time`, `subject` — `room` is nullable. The new insert (`src/app/app/school/StudentSchoolHome.tsx`'s `addTimetableEntry`) supplies exactly `user_id`, `day_of_week`, `subject`, `start_time`, `end_time`, `room: … || null` — every NOT NULL column covered, nothing missing.
- Day-of-week: `DAY_OF_WEEK_OPTIONS` values are `0`(Sun)`..6`(Sat), matching both the DB check constraint and `new Date(...).getDay()`'s native JS convention, which is exactly what `todayDow`/the read-side filter (`timetable.filter((t) => t.day_of_week === todayDow)`) already uses — same convention on both the write and read side, no mismatch.
- Time format: `<input type="time">` produces `HH:MM`, written directly to a Postgres `time` column (accepts it fine). Traced whether a raw-vs-normalized format mismatch could break `todayTimetable`'s `.sort((a,b) => a.start_time.localeCompare(b.start_time))`: the add-class handler never locally splices the raw form value into displayed state — it calls `refetchTimetable()` after insert and only ever renders data that has round-tripped through Supabase/PostgREST (which normalizes `time` columns to `HH:MM:SS` on read), so sort/display always operates on consistently-formatted strings. `formatTime12` also tolerates either format (splits on `:`, only reads the first two segments). Traced end to end, not just "insert succeeds": added-for-today entries appear immediately after the confirm-then-refetch round trip because `newTtDay` defaults to `todayDow`; entries added for a different day correctly wait until that day, which Dev documented as intentional (not a bug — "Today's Classes" was never a weekly view).

**Exam inline date-edit (item 4):**
- `saveExamDate` is a real `.update({ exam_date: editDateValue }).eq("id", examId)`, RLS-scoped per above.
- Traced the edit/cancel state machine: `startEditDate(id, currentDate)` always resets `editDateValue` to the exam's actual current date on open (via the Pencil button), and the inline `<input>` is fully unmounted when `editingDateId !== exam.id` (conditional render). `Cancel` just nulls `editingDateId`; it doesn't need to reset `editDateValue` because the field is unmounted and always re-initialized fresh on next open — no stale/half-edited value is ever visible. Opening a second exam's editor while a first is open correctly swaps to the second (single `editingDateId`/`editDateValue` pair, by design) rather than leaving two open — matches the file's existing `linkingId`-toggle pattern used for "Link a subject."

**CSS Grid safety on `StudentHome.tsx`/`BusinessHome.tsx` (item 5):**
- Grepped every `col-start`/`row-start` in both files post-diff: 6 pairs in `StudentHome.tsx`, 5 in `BusinessHome.tsx`, all still paired, all byte-identical to pre-diff (this diff's hunks never touch those lines). `BusinessPlanHome.tsx`/`BusinessGrowHome.tsx` have zero `col-start` hits — no grid pattern there, zero regression risk of this class.
- Read the actual JSX structure, not just Dev's claim: in both files the error banner `<Card>` sits as a sibling **before** the opening tag of `<div className="... lg:grid ...">`, inside the same non-grid wrapper as the greeting `<h1>`. It is structurally outside the grid, needs no `col-start`/`row-start`, and cannot be misplaced by the grid engine.
- Built an independent Playwright render (not Dev's harness, which was already deleted) — Chromium at `/opt/pw-browsers/chromium-1194`, real compiled CSS pulled fresh from this build's own `.next/static/css` output, static HTML reproducing the exact classNames from the diff for all 6/5 grid cells plus the banner. Rendered 390px and 1280px, dark and light: banner renders full-width above the grid in all 4 combinations, all pinned cells land in their original row/column, no shift, no overlap.

**Error banner UX across the 7 pages (item 6):**
- Read the actual diff for all 7 (`StudentHome`, `BusinessHome`, `StudentSchoolHome`, `BusinessPlanHome`, `BusinessGrowHome`, `deadlines/page`, `exams/page`) — every one uses the identical pre-existing danger-card markup (`border-danger/40` Card, `TriangleAlert` icon, `text-danger` copy), not a per-page reinvention. `error` is confirmed to be typed `string | null` on `useTableRows` (its `setError(err.message)` branch) so `{error}`/`{pageError}` render as plain text, never `"[object Object]"`.
- Independently rendered the exam-card edit/delete UI and the new timetable delete-button + add-class form at 390px/1280px, dark/light (own Playwright harness, not reused from Dev): no overflow, clipping, wrapping, or truncation in any combination.
- **Non-blocking gap found, not introduced by this diff:** `useBusinessProfile` (used by both `BusinessHome.tsx` and `BusinessPlanHome.tsx` for `business_idea`/stage display) is a hand-rolled single-row fetch hook, not built on `useTableRows`, and doesn't track or expose `error` at all — its Supabase error is silently discarded (`const { data: row } = await supabase.from("business_profiles")...`, error destructured away). A fetch failure there is still indistinguishable from "founder hasn't set a business idea yet," which is the exact class of bug Cato's brief targeted, just on a hook that predates `useTableRows` and wasn't named in Cato's Wave 1 scope (which specifically said "wire up `useTableRows`'s error"). Not blocking sign-off — correctly out of the stated scope — but flagging for Cato/Dev as a real residual instance worth a follow-up pass (likely: migrate `useBusinessProfile`/similar single-row hooks onto `useTableRows`'s pattern, or give them their own `error` state).

**Regression check (item 7):** `toggleHomework`, the exam-list `readiness`/`linkedSubject` rendering, and `BusinessPlanHome`'s milestone-toggle logic are all byte-identical pre/post diff (confirmed by reading the full diff hunks — none of these functions appear in any hunk; the diff only adds new functions/JSX around them). No regression.

**Build gates (item 8):** `npx tsc --noEmit` — clean, exit 0, run directly on the merged commit. `npx next build` — clean, exit 0, all 43 routes compiled/prerendered including `/app/school/exams` and `/app/deadlines`, run directly (not taken on Dev's word for either).

**Render check (item 9):** Own Playwright harness built from scratch (Dev's was already deleted per the brief) — real compiled `app.css` copied fresh from this build's `.next/static/css`, static HTML reproducing the actual classNames from the diff. Covered: error banner in the StudentHome/BusinessHome grid context, the exam card's edit/delete UI + inline date editor, the timetable delete button + new add-class form, the homework delete button. All at 390px and 1280px, dark and light (4 combinations each) — no overflow, clipping, misalignment, or theme-specific breakage found in any of them.

**Verdict: SIGN-OFF GIVEN.** This was a real adversarial pass — full diff read (not sampled), schema/grants cross-checked directly against `supabase/schema.sql` (not assumed from the commit message), RLS-only-scoping reasoning verified against the actual policies, edit/cancel state machine traced line by line, timetable day-of-week/time-format round-trip traced end to end including the read side, grid safety re-grepped independently, own tsc/build run, own Playwright renders across both viewports/both themes for every new UI surface. One non-blocking, out-of-scope gap flagged for follow-up (`useBusinessProfile`'s missing `error`) — does not block this pass since it wasn't part of Cato's Wave 1 ask and isn't a regression introduced by this diff.

PROJECT: Alxioum (formerly FutureOS) — student/founder productivity app. Live production app, not greenfield.

GOAL: New-feature initiative. CEO brief this round: "make the agents work on a new feature they think is good" — no feature pre-decided by the CEO; the team picks it. This is a new initiative layered on top of the UI-improvement initiative below (which is complete and shipped) — that section is kept as history, not overwritten.

**Cato's feature pick for this initiative: Deadlines — a unified, cross-track upcoming-deadlines view (exams, homework, business milestones), free-tier, in-app only.** Full reasoning below. This is the CTO brief handed to Product next; Product should treat this as their spec input the same way they treated Cato's UI audit last time (see PRODUCT_SPECS.md for that precedent — Product should write a `PRODUCT_SPECS_DEADLINES.md` or equivalent, not overwrite the old one).

### Why this feature (Cato's reasoning)

**The gap:** Three different tables already carry due-date semantics — `exams.exam_date`, `homework.due_date`, `business_milestones.due_date` — but the product only ever surfaces the single *soonest* item, on Home, with no visual urgency treatment and no way to see the full list anywhere. Worse: `business_milestones.due_date` is a column that has existed in the schema since the business track shipped and is **never read or written by any UI in the codebase** — confirmed by reading `BusinessPlanHome.tsx` in full and grepping the repo. The add-milestone form only ever inserts `title`/`status`/`order_index`; there is no due-date input anywhere, so the column is structurally dead. That's not a hypothetical gap, it's a shipped half-feature.

Today, a student with 3 overdue homework items and an exam in 2 days sees exactly the same Home screen as a student with nothing due — only the single nearest exam and single nearest homework item render, both in the same neutral gray text as everything else on the page, "Passed" (overdue) included. A founder with 5 stale, past-due milestones has literally no way to know it — the column to track that isn't even wired to an input.

**Who it serves:** every user on both tracks, every day — this is the single most-checked kind of information in a productivity app ("what's due, what's late") and today the app under-serves it despite already storing the data. It's also low-friction to notice as missing, which makes it a credible next feature rather than a nice-to-have nobody asked for.

**Why now:** it directly follows the just-shipped UI initiative (EmptyState system, urgency-capable Badge tones `success`/`warning`/`danger` already exist in `globals.css`/`Badge.tsx`, `formatCountdown()` in `src/lib/utils.ts` already returns `"Passed"`/`"Today"`/`"Tomorrow"`/`"N days"`) — the primitives this feature needs are already in place from that pass, so build cost is low and consistency risk is near zero.

**Why this over the other candidates I weighed:**
- *Edit `business_idea`/`stage` after onboarding* — real gap, but single-field, single-track, low breadth. More a settings fix than a feature.
- *Data export (CSV) for founders' metrics/expenses* — real but niche (business-track only, power-user).
- *Coach conversation search/export* — real but narrow; thread history is already reachable via the existing thread popover, search is a nice-to-have on top of a working feature, not a missing one.
- *Achievements leaderboard* — **rejected, not just deprioritized.** A cross-user leaderboard breaks the app's entire data model: every table today is single-user RLS (`using (user_id = auth.uid())`), and a leaderboard requires an aggregate, other-users-visible query — a genuinely new RLS pattern, not a UI addition. It also raises an unaddressed privacy question for a student app whose users may be minors (surfacing names/ranks publicly). This is a multi-week, product-and-legal-reviewed initiative, not a one-pass feature — correctly out of scope per the brief's own sizing guidance.
- *Student study-time logging outside a formal session* — real but student-only and narrower in daily-use value than deadlines, which every user has by definition.

Deadlines wins on user value (both tracks, daily-relevant), architecture fit (reuses existing tables/hooks/design tokens, zero new RLS patterns, zero migrations), and scope (one pass, not a phase).

### Explicit non-goals for this pass
- **No push notifications, no email, no cron.** The environment has no email-sending infra (no Resend/SES in `package.json`) and Render `autoDeploy`/background jobs are already flagged as unreliable in this project's history — building a delivery pipeline is a separate, materially larger initiative (new provider, new secrets, new failure modes) and isn't what makes this gap painful today. In-app surfacing is the actual fix; "notifications" in the CEO's own gap list is being interpreted as in-app for this pass, not push/email.
- **No new database tables or columns.** `exams.exam_date`, `homework.due_date`, and `business_milestones.due_date` already exist and are sufficient. The only data-layer change is making the *existing, unused* `business_milestones.due_date` column actually settable from the UI (a form field addition, not a migration).
- **No new nav tab.** The app has exactly 5 nav tabs on both tracks (`src/lib/navTabs.ts`, `STUDENT_TABS`/`BUSINESS_TABS`, mirrored 1:1) — adding a 6th competes for scarce mobile bottom-nav space for a feature that doesn't need its own primary destination. Follow the existing precedent of `/app/weekly-review` and `/app/upgrade`: real routes, reachable by a link, not in the tab bar.
- **No editing of milestone due dates after creation** in this pass (set at creation only) — an edit affordance is a legitimate follow-up, not required for the core gap.
- **Not Plus-gated.** Every `PaywallGate`-wrapped route today is AI-generation-heavy (Study subsystem: subjects/quizzes/flashcards/progress/weak-topics, and Coach — confirmed via grep of all `PaywallGate` usages). Exams, homework, and Plan milestones are all free-tier CRUD today. Deadlines is a read-only lens over data users already own for free; gating it would be inconsistent with that pattern and would paywall core organizational value, not incremental AI value.
- **No redesign of Home.** The just-shipped, QA-signed-off `StudentHome.tsx`/`BusinessHome.tsx` are not being reopened for a layout rework. At most, Product may spec a small, additive touch (e.g. urgency-tinting the existing single-item tiles, or a "View all" link) — not a restructure.

### Rough shape (Product to turn into a full spec — this is not one)
- **New route:** `/app/deadlines` (or similarly named) — a single page, not a sub-app, listing every open item with a due date across the user's track, grouped by urgency (Overdue / Today / This week / Later), using the existing `Badge` `danger`/`warning`/`success` tones and `formatCountdown()`.
  - Student: merges `exams` (all) + `homework` (`status = 'pending'`) via the already-existing `useExams`/`useHomework` hooks — no new query, no new API route.
  - Business: `business_milestones` where `due_date is not null and status != 'done'`, via the already-existing `useBusinessMilestones` hook.
- **One small form change:** `BusinessPlanHome.tsx`'s add-milestone form gets an optional due-date input, and the insert call starts writing `due_date`. Each milestone card gets a due-date badge when set (reusing `formatCountdown`).
- **Minimal Home touch:** a "View all" link from the existing Home tiles into `/app/deadlines`, and/or urgency-tinting the existing single-item text when overdue/due-soon — Product's call on exact placement, but no structural Home change.
- **No new API routes, no new Supabase tables/migrations, no new AI calls.** This is a pure client-side aggregation + one form-field addition + one new page.

Pipeline from here: Product writes the full spec next (copy, exact layout, grouping thresholds, urgency-badge rules, the Home touch-point design) → Dev builds → QA reviews → Cato signs off, same as the UI initiative.

Two deliberate deviations from Cato's rough shape, called out explicitly in the spec's "What I decided / cut" section (not silent): (1) exams are filtered to `exam_date >= today` rather than "all," since a past exam has no genuine "overdue" state (you can't retroactively take it) — homework/milestones keep real overdue semantics since those can still be completed late; (2) business gets **two** distinct empty states (no milestones at all vs. milestones exist but none are dated) rather than one, because every existing milestone in production has `due_date = null` today — collapsing this into one empty state would tell a founder with real active milestones "nothing here," which is false. Cut for this pass: any in-place action on the Deadlines page itself (tap-through/read-only only — exams have no completion concept, so a partial inline-action list would be inconsistent), filter/sort controls (too little data at this stage to justify), drag-to-reschedule/snooze (no due-date editing after creation per Cato's brief, no notification infra to snooze against), and a redundant "Deadlines" section heading on Home (the button copy already carries it).

## UI/UX IMPROVEMENT INITIATIVE (COMPLETE — history, not active)

GOAL: UI/UX improvement initiative on the existing live app. CEO brief: "Use the team to make Alxioum better, use Product to make the UI a lot better — fix it." Building is authorized now, not just planning. Scope: visual polish, consistency, and UX gaps in the existing screens — not new features, not a redesign of the architecture.

MVP: N/A — post-MVP, live app with paying users on two locked tracks (student, business).

FEATURES: Student track: Home, School (Subjects/Exams/Flashcards/Quizzes/Progress study system), Future (career matches + roadmap), Coach (AI chat), Profile (XP/achievements/skills). Business track: Home, Plan (milestones), Grow (metrics/expenses/content helper/competitors), Coach, Profile. Shared: onboarding + /choose-plan (track lock), Stripe billing + PaywallGate, missions, weekly review.

TECH STACK: Next.js 15 (App Router) + TypeScript + Tailwind. Supabase (Postgres + Auth). Stripe. Anthropic Claude API. Hosted on Render (https://alxioum-app.onrender.com). Design tokens via CSS custom properties in globals.css (dark-first, `.light` override class), gradient/glass utility classes, RadialStat/StatTile/Card/Badge/ProgressBar as the shared visual primitives.

ARCHITECTURE: Two product tracks (student, business) chosen once at /choose-plan and permanently locked by a Postgres trigger once onboarding_completed is true. Route-level branching pattern: shared routes (`/app`, `/app/school`, `/app/future`) render a track-specific component (e.g. `StudentHome`/`BusinessHome`, `StudentSchoolHome`/`BusinessPlanHome`, `StudentFutureHome`/`BusinessGrowHome`) chosen by `profile.track` in a thin page/layout wrapper. Coach and Profile are track-agnostic single implementations with inline conditional copy. Nav (SidebarNav desktop, BottomNav mobile) is driven by one shared `STUDENT_TABS`/`BUSINESS_TABS` table in `src/lib/navTabs.ts`. The student School tab has a real sub-app (SchoolSubNav: Home/Subjects/Exams/Flashcards/Quizzes/Progress); the business Plan tab is intentionally a single flat page with no sub-nav (by design, per code comment in `school/layout.tsx`) — this is the root cause of most business-track UI thinness identified below.

## QA LIVE-DATA PASS (production Supabase, Deadlines feature) — 2026-08-27

CEO explicitly authorized this pass: seed real, disposable rows directly in production Supabase (project `bgfhcpegsdyxpvmdxkuc`) via the Supabase MCP connector, verify real behavior, then delete everything and confirm cleanup. Every prior QA pass on this project was code-level/static-render only; this is the first pass with real DB access. Methodology and findings only below — no seeded emails, UUIDs, or secrets recorded, per instruction.

**Method:** created two disposable `auth.users` + `profiles` rows (obviously-fake `@example.invalid` addresses, one per track, `onboarding_completed = true` set directly so the real onboarding/auth flow didn't need to be driven). Read `BusinessPlanHome.tsx`'s actual `addMilestone` insert call and matched its exact column set/values with a plain SQL `insert` (no `.upsert()` anywhere in that path — confirmed again by reading the file, matching the prior code-level QA finding). Seeded 4 exams + 4 homework rows on the student account (past/today/+3d/+20d exams; overdue-pending/today-pending/+5d-pending/completed homework) and 6 milestones on the business account, including the two adversarial cases the spec cares about most: `due_date = null`, and a `status = 'done'` milestone with a past `due_date`.

**Deadlines logic traced by hand against the real rows (not eyeballed — actual SQL run, actual output shown):**
- Student: reproduced `exams.filter(daysBetween(today, exam_date) >= 0)` + `homework.filter(status === 'pending')` as SQL. Result: 6 items — past exam correctly excluded entirely (not even bucketed "overdue", matching the documented, deliberate spec deviation that exams have no overdue state); today's exam+homework both bucketed "today"; +3d exam and +5d homework both bucketed "this-week"; +20d exam bucketed "later"; overdue homework correctly bucketed "overdue"; the `completed`-status homework was correctly excluded regardless of its due date.
- Business: reproduced `milestones.filter(due_date != null && status !== 'done')` as SQL. Result: exactly 4 items (overdue/today/this-week/later, one per bucket, thresholds matching `bucketForDate` exactly: diff -7/0/+4/+14). **The highest-risk case held**: the `done` milestone with a past due date (diff -2, would have bucketed "overdue" if leaked) was correctly excluded, and the null-due-date milestone was correctly excluded. No bug found here — this is the one Product/Dev were most likely to get wrong and it's correct in production.

**RLS — checked live `pg_policies`, not just repo `schema.sql` (no drift found):** `business_milestones_all_own`, `exams_all_own`, `homework_all_own` are all `FOR ALL ... USING (user_id = auth.uid()) WITH CHECK (user_id = auth.uid())`; `profiles` has separate `id = auth.uid()` policies for select/insert/update. Went further than a policy-text read: impersonated the disposable student account in-session (`set local role authenticated; set local "request.jwt.claims"`) and ran real queries as that role — confirmed 0 rows of the other account's `business_milestones` visible, 0 rows of the other account's `homework` visible, and a real cross-account `UPDATE` attempt against the business account's milestone affected 0 rows. Also attempted a real self-upgrade (`update profiles set plan = 'plus', plan_status = 'active'` as the impersonated student): rejected with a Postgres `42501 permission denied for table profiles`, confirming the column-restricted `UPDATE` grant is live and enforced, not just present in `schema.sql`. Live `authenticated` UPDATE grant on `profiles` matches the repo's intended column list exactly (billing columns `plan`/`plan_status`/`trial_ends_at`/`stripe_customer_id`/`stripe_subscription_id` excluded).
- Minor hygiene note, not a live vulnerability: `anon` still carries the default Supabase blanket `UPDATE` grant on all `profiles` columns (including billing) — this is standard/default for every table in a fresh Supabase project and is inert today because no `profiles` policy targets `anon` (RLS denies it 0 rows regardless of column grant). Flagged for Cato as defense-in-depth hygiene, not urgent: worth an explicit `revoke update on public.profiles from anon;` so a future policy change can't accidentally combine with this pre-existing grant.

**`get_advisors` (security + performance) — run for the first time on this project:**
- Security (3 WARN): (1)+(2) `public.prevent_track_change_after_onboarding()` (the track-lock trigger function) is `SECURITY DEFINER` and technically callable by `anon`/`authenticated` via `/rest/v1/rpc/...`. **Verified directly, not assumed**: called it as `authenticated` and got `ERROR 0A000: trigger functions can only be called as triggers` — Postgres itself blocks direct invocation outside trigger context, so this is not exploitable today. Still worth a one-line `REVOKE EXECUTE` for hygiene/to silence the advisor, flagged to Cato/Dev as low-priority. (3) Leaked-password protection (HaveIBeenPwned check) is disabled in Auth settings — genuine, real gap, flagged to Cato: cheap to turn on, no downside found.
- Performance (59 findings, none CRITICAL): 34 `auth_rls_initplan` WARNs — every single `_all_own` RLS policy in the schema (including `business_milestones`, `exams`, `homework`, all 3 `profiles` policies) re-evaluates `auth.uid()` per-row instead of `(select auth.uid())`, which is the standard Supabase perf footgun at scale. This is systemic across the whole schema, not specific to the Deadlines feature — flagged to Cato as a backlog item (rewriting every `_all_own` policy), not a Deadlines-blocking bug. 21 `unindexed_foreign_keys` (INFO) including `exams.study_subject_id_fkey`. 4 `unused_index` (INFO).

**Cleanup — verified, not assumed:** deleted all seeded rows (`exams`, `homework`, `business_milestones`, `profiles`, `auth.users`) for both disposable accounts, then ran a follow-up `SELECT` count against every one of those 5 tables filtered by the disposable UUIDs, plus a `LIKE 'qa-test-%@example.invalid'` sweep across `auth.users` to catch anything the UUID filter might have missed. All 6 counts returned `0`. No disposable data remains in production.

**Verdict:** Deadlines' core urgency-bucketing and cross-source filtering logic is correct against real data, including the adversarial `done`-with-past-due-date case. RLS isolation (including write-path and the billing-column lockdown) held up under live, real impersonation attempts, not just a policy-text read. No blocking bugs found. Two non-blocking items handed to Cato: (1) schema-wide `auth_rls_initplan` perf cleanup (systemic, pre-existing, not introduced by Deadlines), (2) leaked-password protection toggle + `SECURITY DEFINER` EXECUTE-revoke hygiene on the track-lock trigger function (verified not currently exploitable).

  regressions — they were simply never surfaced by a prior pass scoped to a single feature):
  1. **XP-farming via toggle** — `toggleHomework`/`toggleMilestone` award XP on every completion, no
     once-only guard (unlike `completeStudySession`, which has one). Low security impact, real product bug.

     Postgres doesn't guarantee tie order on `logged_date` alone. Exactly the "2+ same-key entries" case
     the task asked me to check, and it's broken.

     this blocked me from literally completing that checklist item, live or otherwise. Business track's
     equivalent (add-milestone) is fine. Scope question for Cato/Product, not a Dev bug fix.

  2026-08-27 live pass on byte-identical schema (high confidence, not fresh evidence this session).
- No cleanup needed — the network block meant no write ever reached the database this session.

## UI PREMIUM VISUAL PASS (Product, isolated worktree — parallel with Dev's codebase audit and QA's full-app test)

CEO brief: make the UI look genuinely premium/professional-caliber ("came from 100,000 people web development/app development"), not incremental tweaks. Mandated scope strategy: tokens first (`globals.css`/`tailwind.config.ts`), then shared primitives, only bespoke screens if the uplift doesn't already cover it. Figma/Canva authorized; Lovable explicitly out of scope (separate hosted app, can't edit this codebase).

**Figma/Canva note (No-BS):** neither tool was actually exposed as a callable tool in this execution session (only Read/Write/Edit/Bash/Glob/Grep/WebSearch were available) despite being authorized. Did not fabricate a Figma file/link. Did the design-system work directly in tokens with real Playwright render-harness verification substituting for a Figma mockup review. No genuine Canva need arose — every icon use is already covered by Lucide.

**What shipped (8 files, tokens + primitives only, no screen restructuring):** `src/app/globals.css` + `tailwind.config.ts` — multi-layer elevation/shadow system replacing single soft-blur shadows, light-mode `--shadow-color` deliberately enriched (light has almost no luminance gap between card and page to lean on), new global `:focus-visible` halo ring (previously **zero** focus states existed anywhere in the app — grepped, confirmed), `.glass` refinement (blur/saturate/border), `text-wrap: balance` on headings. `Card.tsx`/`ProgressBar.tsx`/`RadialStat.tsx`/`StatTile.tsx` — transition-shadow, inset track groove, stronger ring track color, `tabular-nums`. Two 1-line mechanical touches in `StudentHome.tsx`/`BusinessHome.tsx` swapping an existing local `HOVER_LIFT` constant's shadow token (no grid/structural changes — `col-start`/`row-start` grepped identical before/after).

**Real bug caught mid-pass, not just a design choice:** `.glass`'s own `box-shadow` was silently overriding the `shadow-card` Tailwind utility applied alongside it on every `Card` (same CSS property, `.glass` sits later in the compiled stylesheet, so it always won) — the app's card elevation was effectively half-dead before this pass; no shadow-token change would have been visible until this was found and fixed (moved the glass "top-edge highlight" into the shadow tokens themselves via a new `--card-highlight` variable instead of `.glass` owning `box-shadow`).

**Deferred, with reasoning (see `UI_REDESIGN_REPORT.md`):** typography scale consolidation (49 arbitrary `text-[Npx]` usages across ~30 files — real but too broad/low-value for one pass), border-radius scale (large-radius language is consistent and part of existing brand identity, not a bug), any screen-level redesign beyond the two mechanical `HOVER_LIFT` touches (the token+primitive uplift alone visibly fixed the biggest issue — flat, undefined light-mode cards — on every screenshotted screen).

**Verification:** no live Supabase session in this worktree, so built a real-component render harness (not hand-approximated HTML) — bundled the actual `Card`/`Button`/`Badge`/`ProgressBar`/`RadialStat`/`StatTile`/`MissionHeroCard`/`EmptyState`/`ScreenHeader`/real Lucide icons/real `cn`/`deadlines` lib via esbuild with mock data, rendered via `react-dom/server`, compiled through the actual `tailwind.config.ts`/`globals.css`, screenshotted with Playwright/Chromium at 390px/1280px, light/dark, before/after, for Home (both tracks), School/Exams, Deadlines, Coach — same harness methodology QA used in the prior UI initiative. Also verified `:focus-visible` with real keyboard Tab navigation (`document.activeElement` + screenshot), not just visual inspection. Harness lived in a git-ignored `.harness-tmp/` at repo root, deleted before final verification — `git status` confirmed clean apart from the 8 intentional source files. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx next build` both clean (all 42 routes) after harness deletion.

**Not done in this pass:** functional/data/route changes (none — pure visual pass, confirmed via `git diff` file list), Dev-side implementation review, QA sign-off (both out of scope for this isolated worktree; Cato reconciles across all three parallel worktrees).

DEPLOYMENT STATUS (this pass): not deployed — isolated worktree, awaiting Cato's reconciliation across the parallel Dev/QA/Product worktrees before merge.

1. **XP-farming (`toggleHomework`/`toggleMilestone`, no once-only guard) — fixed.** Applied the same one-way-completion guard `completeStudySession` already uses: both functions now early-return once the item is already completed/done, and the "mark as pending"/"mark as not done" undo affordance is removed (button disabled once complete, matching `completeStudySession`'s exact UX). This is a deliberate call to make completion semantics consistent across all three XP-awarding actions in the app rather than adding a new `xp_awarded_at` column to preserve toggle-back — zero migration, zero new failure surface, closes the exploit completely. Files: `src/app/app/school/StudentSchoolHome.tsx`, `src/app/app/school/BusinessPlanHome.tsx`.
2. **Business metrics trend badge (no tiebreaker on same-day entries) — fixed.** Extended `useTableRows`'s `orderBy` option to accept an array of clauses (backward compatible, single-clause callers unchanged), and added `created_at desc` as a secondary sort key to `useBusinessMetrics`. Same-day same-key entries now resolve deterministically to insertion order. Files: `src/lib/hooks/useTableRows.ts`, `src/lib/hooks/domain.ts`.

Business accounts get a "Plan" card (milestones completed all-time, expenses logged this week — added `useBusinessExpenses` to the page) in place of the "School" card, and `nextFocus` now generates milestone-based copy for business instead of exam/homework language that could never fire for that track. `weekly_reviews` has no business-specific columns, so the existing generic int columns (`assignments_completed`, `study_minutes`) are reused per-track (documented inline) rather than adding new columns — no migration. Both are plain `.insert()` calls, no new tables/columns, same pattern `BusinessPlanHome.addMilestone` already established. Business track already had this (add-milestone), so this closes the last remaining track asymmetry of this class.
