## UI PREMIUM VISUAL PASS — ROUND 2 (Product, worktree `agent-a4979b1aa14970469`)

Picked up the two things round 1 explicitly deferred (typography-scale consolidation, screen-level polish on the newest UI) plus a re-check of Figma/Canva availability. Full detail in `UI_REDESIGN_REPORT.md` (round 2 section, appended). Short version:

- **Figma/Canva: still not exposed as callable tools this session** (same honest finding as round 1) — proceeded in-code with Playwright verification, no fabrication.
- **Typography scale: consolidated, not just deferred.** Re-audited the 51 arbitrary `text-[Npx]` usages (30 files) and found they were already a de facto, repeated scale (11px alone was 22 of 51, always the same "eyebrow" role) rather than true noise — so instead of a risky broad rewrite, added 9 named `fontSize` tokens to `tailwind.config.ts` (`2xs`/`caption`/`tooltip`/`label`/`body`/`title`/`title-lg`/`heading`/`display`) that are byte-for-byte the same pixel values, and mechanically renamed every usage to the matching token. Zero visual diff, zero regression risk (renamed, not resized) — confirmed via `grep` returning zero remaining `text-[Npx]` matches and matching per-token counts. One value (14px) needed no new token since it was already identical to Tailwind's own default `text-sm`.
- **Real regression caught and fixed: round 1's `.glass`/`shadow-card` fix left 25 `glass` elements across 5 files with zero box-shadow.** Round 1 correctly moved `box-shadow` out of `.glass` and into paired `shadow-*` utilities, but never audited every existing `glass` call site for whether it actually had one — it didn't, in 25 places: the entire Coach page, the subject-session AI tutor page (14 separate blocks), the homework-help chat page, and 2 onboarding result cards. This was invisible in round 1 because that pass's screenshots covered Home/Deadlines/School-Exams, not Coach — caught this round specifically because Coach was in scope. Fixed by adding a role-appropriate `shadow-subtle`/`shadow-card`/`shadow-raised` to all 25 sites.
- **Screen-level polish on the two brand-new forms from the last reconciliation** (`StudentSchoolHome.tsx` add-homework, `exams/page.tsx` add-exam — both added by Cato to close QA's "no add-exam/homework UI" finding, neither had a design pass). Found a real mobile bug: the homework form crammed 4 elements (subject/title/date/submit) into one flex row, leaving the title input roughly 40-50px wide at 390px — broken, not just tight. Restructured both to two-row layouts matching the visual grammar `BusinessPlanHome`'s existing add-milestone form already established (max one `flex-1` per row). No new components, no functional changes — pure DOM re-grouping of existing inputs.
- **Considered and explicitly rejected:** urgency-tinting the Deadlines page's source-type icon circles — the urgency signal is already carried by the badge tone and section header; a third color signal would be redundant and would compete with the icon's existing "item type" role. Left unchanged.
- **Verification:** same real-component Playwright-harness methodology as round 1 (esbuild-bundled real components + hand-copied verbatim classNames, compiled through the actual `tailwind.config.ts`/`globals.css` via the Tailwind CLI, screenshotted at 390px/1280px × light/dark). Confirmed Coach's cards/bubbles/panel now read as lifted in both themes (previously flat), both redesigned forms have comfortable input widths at 390px, and the new typography scale renders as a coherent hierarchy with no size regressions. Harness lived in git-ignored `.harness-tmp/`, deleted before commit.
- **Build gates:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean, `npx next build` clean (all 42 routes), both run directly after harness deletion.
- **Grid safety / mobile / both themes / no functional changes:** all re-confirmed the same way as round 1 (see `UI_REDESIGN_REPORT.md` for the full checklist) — `col-start`/`row-start` pairing on `StudentHome.tsx`/`BusinessHome.tsx` untouched by this round's edits.

Files changed (round 2): `tailwind.config.ts` (new fontSize scale) + 33 `.tsx` files (typography-token rename, `shadow-*` additions to previously shadow-less `glass` elements, and the two form restructures). Full list via `git diff --stat` on commit noted below.

## CATO — RECONCILIATION OF 3 PARALLEL ROUND-2 WORKTREES (Dev audit, Product visual pass, QA overflow bug) — 2026-08-28

CEO ran a second round of Dev/Product/QA in parallel isolated worktrees off `acab9ef`. This section is the CTO merge/reconcile/fix/sign-off pass across all three.

**Merge sequence:** Dev (`worktree-agent-a554984a31f5d5ad2`, `26bef55`) merged clean, no conflict (report-only, `DEV_AUDIT_REPORT.md` only). Product (`worktree-agent-a4979b1aa14970469`, `a23c540`) merged clean, no conflict (touched 36 files, but no overlap with Dev's report-only commit). QA (`worktree-agent-a1c15912007ddcf99`, `34204f1`) conflicted only on `PROJECT_STATE.md` — Product's and QA's round-2 sections were both pure appends at the same insertion point, not overlapping edits to the same prose — resolved by keeping both sections in full, separated by a `---` divider, nothing dropped or summarized. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx next build` both clean (42 routes) immediately after all three merges, before any further changes (commit `99cf2ca`).

**What each agent did this round:** Dev re-verified round 1's fixes against the pre-fix diffs (not just the after-state) and checked all 17 `useTableRows` callers — zero new bugs, report-only. Product consolidated 51 arbitrary `text-[Npx]` usages into 9 named Tailwind typography tokens (byte-identical values, pure rename, zero visual diff) and — independently, in the same pass — caught and fixed its own round-1 regression: 25 `.glass` call sites across Coach, the AI-tutor chat, homework-help, and onboarding-results that never got a paired `shadow-*` utility after round 1 moved `box-shadow` out of `.glass`, silently going visually flat. Product also reflowed the two brand-new add-exam/add-homework forms (added last round to close QA's round-1 Bug 4) from one cramped row into two, independently of QA's parallel investigation into the same forms. QA re-confirmed all 4 of round 1's fixes hold, again could not reach live Supabase (network-blocked, confirmed 3 ways again), and built an unauthenticated Playwright harness against the real dev server + real compiled CSS at real phone widths (320–390px) — finding a genuine mobile-breaking bug in the same two new forms Product had just touched.

**The overflow bug — verified against the actual merged code, not QA's pre-merge description.** QA's report (written in an isolated worktree, before merge) describes the bug against the *original* single-row, 4-element form layout, where a missing `min-w-0` on the flex-1 title/subject input, combined with 2+ flex-basis-auto siblings each defaulting to `min-width: auto` (browser default ~170-200px per bare `<input>`), summed to far more than the ~340px of available width at phone widths — pushing the date input and submit button off-screen with the app's pre-existing `overflow-x: hidden` on `html`/`body` making them unreachable. By the time both branches were merged, Product's independent two-row reflow (landed in the same round, unrelated motivation — visual polish, not this bug) had already changed the form shape: each row now holds at most one `flex-1` element next to fixed-width siblings, and I confirmed via a real Playwright render harness (esbuild-free, exact copy-pasted JSX/classNames from the actual merged files, served through the real Next.js dev server, real compiled Tailwind, screenshotted at 320/375/390px) that the severe off-screen failure QA found **no longer reproduces** in the merged two-row layout, with or without `min-w-0` — `document.documentElement.scrollWidth` equals `clientWidth` at all three widths either way.

That does not make QA's finding wrong or the fix unnecessary — it means the fix target moved. `min-w-0` was still missing from 4 flex-1 inputs across both forms (title in the homework form's row 1, and the date input in each form's row 2) where every *other* flex-1 element in these same files correctly has it, matching an established codebase invariant, and it's a zero-risk, zero-visual-diff, one-line defensive addition against exactly the failure class QA identified (long unbroken content, a future crowded-row edit, or a narrower device than tested) even though it isn't live-breaking today. Applied `min-w-0` to all 4: `src/app/app/school/exams/page.tsx` (date input, add-exam form) and `src/app/app/school/StudentSchoolHome.tsx` (title input row 1, date input row 2, add-homework form).

**Verification, not assumption:** rendered both forms through a temporary route (`src/app/harness-verify-xyz123/`, JSX copy-pasted verbatim from the real merged source, deleted before commit — confirmed absent via `git status` and a re-run `npx next build` showing all 42 routes with no trace of it) via the real `npm run dev` server. Reproduced the pre-fix state first (removed `min-w-0` from the homework-form title input only, matching QA's literal finding) and confirmed even that narrower repro did not overflow post-reflow (223-244px title width, comfortable) — establishing that Product's reflow, not the `min-w-0` fix, was what closed the severe failure QA hit. Then re-applied the fix and confirmed via Playwright `boundingBox()` on the date input and submit button (all 4 combinations of {exam-form, hw-form} × verified fully within `[0, viewportWidth]`) and `document.documentElement.scrollWidth === clientWidth` at 320/375/390px that there is no horizontal page overflow in the final merged+fixed state. Screenshot confirms both forms render cleanly, all controls visible, no clipping.

**Secondary finding (no `min` date guard on exams, no exam edit/delete) — fixed the cheap part, deferred the rest.** Added `min={todayISO()}` (already imported/available via `src/lib/utils.ts`) to both the exam date input and, for the same reason and same one-line safety, the homework due-date input — a native HTML constraint that blocks picking a past date in the date-picker UI and triggers form-level validation on submit. This is a soft client-side guard only (not a server-side/RLS constraint), which is the right size for what's actually a UX footgun, not a security issue. **Deferring exam edit/delete UI** — real gap (a still-possible bad date, e.g. via manual keyboard entry bypassing the picker, becomes a permanently stuck tile), but it's a materially bigger surface (new UI affordance, new interaction pattern, decisions about undo/confirm) than this round's scope, and homework already has a partial escape hatch (mark-as-completed) that exams structurally lack today by design (exams have no completion concept per the original Deadlines-initiative decision) — building real exam mutation UI is better sized as its own small task than bundled into a bug-fix round.

**Final verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean (exit 0) and `npx next build` clean (exit 0, all 42 routes) after the merge and after the fix, run directly — not taken on any agent's word. `.next` cache was cleared once mid-session after a stale generated-types reference to the deleted harness route surfaced a transient `tsc` error; re-run confirmed clean.

BLOCKERS: None.

NEXT TASK: None open from this round. Recommend exam edit/delete UI as a right-sized standalone task if the CEO wants to close the deferred secondary gap, and the next QA pass still needs live Supabase access restored (unchanged recommendation from round 1's reconciliation — this round's network block was independently re-confirmed 3 ways, same as round 1).

## UI PREMIUM VISUAL PASS — ROUND 3 (Product, worktree `agent-a75f9963c88db1c5d`, full tool access) — 2026-08-29

**Trigger:** rounds 1–2 shipped real token/shadow/typography/focus-state work, but a real human tester's verdict came back unchanged from before any of it: *"the ui looks vibe coded and just looks bad."* This round had full tool access (Figma, Canva, unrestricted WebSearch) — deliberately different from rounds 1–2, which ran as the constrained `product` subagent (Read/Write/Edit/Bash/Glob/Grep/WebSearch only) despite Figma/Canva being nominally authorized both times.

**Diagnosis (screenshotted, not assumed):** the problem was never the tokens — it was composition. Rendered every pre-app screen with Playwright (real `next dev` for landing/login/signup; a real-component esbuild+Tailwind-CLI harness, same methodology as rounds 1–2, for the authenticated screens no live Supabase session in this sandbox can reach). At 1280px, landing/login/signup/every onboarding question step were a mobile-width column dead-centered in a huge empty desktop canvas — zero `lg:` treatment anywhere in those files. Home dashboards had a real but less severe version of the same issue (column-height mismatch in the `lg:grid-cols-[minmax(0,1fr)_320px]` layout — partially mitigated in production by `/app/layout.tsx`'s existing max-width cap, sidebar, and ambient-glow blobs, which the harness doesn't render). Brand gradient usage was thin (one headline word + one button on landing) and the icon-in-rounded-square+chevron card was the dominant, repeated composition primitive on both dashboards. Two `WebSearch` queries against current design-quality sources named these as specific, common "AI slop" tells (centered hero + one CTA, purple-indigo gradient confined to small spots, identical icon-card grids) — used to sharpen the diagnosis into a checkable list, not to copy any competitor's screen. Full detail, including the exact screenshots' findings and sources cited, in `UI_REDESIGN_REPORT.md`'s Round 3 section.

**Figma: genuinely available and used this time**, unlike rounds 1–2's honest "not exposed" finding. Built two real reference frames in a real Figma file (asymmetric two-column landing + onboarding-question composition, `BrandPanel`-style right-hand visual anchor instead of dead space) and used them as the direct layout reference for the code changes below. Canva: no genuine need arose (same as rounds 1–2 — every visual need is covered by CSS gradients/SVG/Lucide, no raster asset requirement).

**What shipped (composition-level, not just tokens — new authorization this round):** new shared `src/components/shared/BrandPanel.tsx` (desktop-only, 5 copy variants) plus `lg:` two-column restructuring on landing (`page.tsx`), login/signup, and `OnboardingShell.tsx` (fixes all 18 onboarding question steps via the one shared shell) — every one of these previously had zero desktop composition. Landing also got a real proof strip (checkable claims, not invented stats) and a sharper, Alxioum-specific subcopy rewrite. Home dashboards (`StudentHome.tsx`/`BusinessHome.tsx`) got column-height rebalancing only — 4 existing cards total moved to different `row-start` values across both files, zero new/removed cards, zero data changes — narrowing the column-height gap from ~380px to ~30px (Student) and ~380px to ~115px (Business) in the harness. No functional/data/route changes anywhere — confirmed via `git diff`, every change is JSX structure, className, or copy.

**Caught and fixed mid-round, not left in:** a mobile regression the landing proof-strip change introduced (`truncate` clipping the longer proof values illegibly at 390px) — caught by the mandatory mobile-regression screenshot pass, fixed, re-verified.

**Deliberately not done, with reasoning:** the two onboarding `ResultsScreen`s (already show real content, lower leverage than the empty-canvas question screens); the icon-in-rounded-square card primitive repeated across both Home dashboards (real finding, but fixing it app-wide is a materially bigger, riskier change than a composition pass and risks conflicting with Cato's standing "no Home redesign" direction if pushed further than the column-rebalancing done here — flagged as the best candidate for a dedicated future round); `choose-plan/page.tsx` (outside this round's named scope). Rounds 1–2's token/shadow/typography/focus-state/`.glass` work was kept and built on throughout, not replaced.

**Verification:** before/after Playwright screenshots, both themes, both viewports (390px/1280px), for every screen touched — landing, login, signup, StudentHome, BusinessHome, StudentOnboarding (representative of all 18 onboarding steps). Explicit mobile-regression pass on all 6 screens after every change. `col-start`/`row-start` re-grepped after the Home edits — still every grid item paired, none orphaned. `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx next build` both clean (exit 0, all 42 routes) after harness deletion, run directly.

## DEV — DIRECT AI HOMEWORK HELP (per homework item), worktree `agent-a6c727fbe8e3c9a6e` — 2026-08-31

CEO asked for the AI to help students finish their actual homework, directly. Cato scoped it first and found the existing AI tutor (`/api/study/tutor` + `subjects/[subjectId]/homework-help/page.tsx`) is a dead end for this: it requires a real `study_subjects` row, but `homework` items only carry a free-text `subject` string with no link to `study_subjects`. Built a new, homework-native path instead of forcing a reuse.

**Shipped:**
- `src/app/api/school/homework-help/route.ts` — new route, closely mirrors `/api/study/tutor`'s structure and security pattern (`requireUser` → `checkEntitlement` (Plus-gated, 402 if not) → `ANTHROPIC_API_KEY` check (503 if missing) → body validation (400) → DB lookup). Resolves the homework row itself from `homework` scoped to `.eq("id", homeworkId).eq("user_id", user.id)` — never trusts a client-supplied subject/title — 404 if not found/not owned. System prompt adapts the tutor's teach-don't-just-answer philosophy, grounded in `homework.subject` + `.title` + `.priority` instead of a study_subject/topic. Reuses `callStudyAIForText` at the same `maxTokens: 900, effort: "low"` as the existing tutor. 502 on AI failure.
- `src/app/app/school/homework/[homeworkId]/help/page.tsx` — same UX pattern as the subject-based homework-help page (4 quick-action buttons: Hint/Explain/Example/Walkthrough, back link, chat-bubble rendering, "teaches the concept, won't just hand you the answer" footer). Loads the homework item via the existing `useHomework` hook, filtered client-side by `homeworkId` (no new dedicated single-row query needed — list is already small and cached per user). Shows subject/title/priority as the page header instead of requiring the student to retype the question; quick actions frame generically ("Give me a hint to get started on this homework") when the optional textarea is left blank.
- `src/app/app/school/homework/layout.tsx` — new `PaywallGate` wrapper scoped to this one subtree only, matching the exact pattern used by `subjects/`, `weak-topics/`, `quizzes/`, `progress/`, `flashcards/`. Does **not** touch `/app/school` itself (the homework list/CRUD stays free-tier).
- `src/app/app/school/StudentSchoolHome.tsx` — added a compact circular "AI help" icon button (Sparkles icon, `h-8 w-8`, same visual weight as the existing complete-toggle checkbox) next to `PriorityBadge` on each pending homework row, linking to `/app/school/homework/${hw.id}/help`. Nothing else in the card restructured.

**Scope decisions:**
- **No chat persistence.** Deliberately ephemeral — no new `homework_help_messages` table, no migration. Each request only carries the current message, not prior turns (the API body is `{ homeworkId, message }`, no history array). This matches the CEO's constraint of no new DB tables/columns and keeps the surface identical to the quick-action-button UX (each click is already a fresh, self-contained question). Trade-off: a student can't say "explain that differently" and have the AI recall its last reply within this route — flagging this now rather than silently deciding it doesn't matter, per instructions. If back-and-forth threading turns out to matter, the fix is a `study_tutor_messages`-shaped table plus a `homeworkId`/`homework_id` history read, exactly mirroring the existing tutor route.
- **Plus-gated**, consistent with every other AI-generation route in the app (free tier is CRUD-only). Wrapped via a route-scoped `PaywallGate` layout, not an ad hoc check in the page, so a non-Plus user sees the standard upgrade-activation screen instead of a raw 402.
- **Icon-only entry point, not a text pill.** Built a text+icon "AI help" pill first, but a mobile Playwright render (harness route, deleted before commit) showed it squeezed the flex-1 `subject: title` text into much harder truncation than before at narrow widths (320–390px) on cards with long combined subject+title strings — a regression risk in a codebase QA has flagged for exactly this overflow/truncation failure class before. Confirmed the truncation itself is pre-existing (same long-title stress case truncates hard even with zero new UI, just less so), then switched to a compact `h-8 w-8` icon-only circular button matching the checkbox's visual weight, which minimizes the width taken from the title.

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx next build` both clean (exit 0, all 43 routes, both run twice — once before, once after clearing a stale `.next` generated-types reference to the deleted harness route, same known artifact class as round 2's reconciliation). Rendered the new page + updated homework-card entry point through a real Playwright browser (`/opt/pw-browsers/chromium`) against a temporary unauthenticated harness route (exact JSX/classNames copy-pasted from the real merged files, static mock data, no live Supabase available in this sandbox) at 320px/390px/1280px — zero horizontal overflow (`scrollWidth === clientWidth`) at every width, harness route confirmed fully deleted via `git status` and a clean re-grep for its name across `src` and `.next`. `col-start`/`row-start` grepped clean across every touched file (none use CSS Grid — this feature never touches the Home dashboards' grid layout).

BLOCKERS: None.

NEXT TASK: None open from this task. If usage data later shows students wanting multi-turn threading on this screen (not just single-shot quick actions), the natural follow-up is a small migration adding a `homework_help_messages` table mirroring `study_tutor_messages`'s shape — flagged above, not built now per the no-new-migration constraint.

## QA REVIEW — DIRECT AI HOMEWORK HELP (Dev's commit `683cbbe`) — 2026-08-31

Reviewed adversarially against the actual shipped code, not Dev's summary. This worktree started at `b73ec8a` (did not have Dev's commit); pulled Dev's 5 files in via `git checkout 683cbbe -- <paths>` (content-identical to `683cbbe`, confirmed via `git diff 683cbbe --stat` returning empty for all 4 app files) rather than a branch merge, so this worktree's own commit carries the reviewed code forward for Cato to reconcile.

**Read `git show 683cbbe` in full (5 files, +293/-1) — not sampled.**

**Security (the core thing that matters here) — holds up.** `src/app/api/school/homework-help/route.ts`'s DB lookup is `client.from("homework").select("subject, title, priority").eq("id", homeworkId).eq("user_id", user.id).maybeSingle()` — genuinely scoped, not just "id" alone. `client` comes from `requireUser(req)` → `supabaseForRequest(req)`, a user-scoped client that carries the caller's JWT (traced `src/lib/supabase/server.ts` in full) — not the service-role client (`src/lib/supabase/server.ts` documents that privileged client is reserved, unused by any route today, confirmed by reading it). So even if the `.eq("user_id", ...)` filter were ever dropped by mistake, RLS is a second, independent backstop. `homework`'s live policy is the standard `homework_all_own` (`for all to authenticated using (user_id = auth.uid()) with check (user_id = auth.uid())`) in `supabase/schema.sql:127`, and this diff touches zero schema/migration/grant files, so nothing here could have weakened it. Did **not** re-run a live impersonation test this round (no Supabase MCP tool was exposed in my available toolset this session, and this diff makes no schema/policy change) — carrying forward the 2026-08-27 live pass's direct impersonation-tested confirmation that `_all_own` policies including cross-account isolation hold in production, on unchanged schema. A user requesting help for a `homeworkId` they don't own gets a clean 404 (`maybeSingle()` returns null → `{error: "Homework not found."}`, 404), not another user's data.

**Paywall gating — genuine, two independent layers, both verified.** `src/app/app/school/homework/layout.tsx` wraps `children` in `<PaywallGate>`, byte-identical pattern to `subjects/layout.tsx`, `weak-topics/layout.tsx`, `quizzes/layout.tsx`, `progress/layout.tsx`, `flashcards/layout.tsx`, `coach/layout.tsx` (grepped all 8 `PaywallGate` usages in the repo). Read `PaywallGate.tsx` itself: `isEntitled(profile)` gates on `plan_status === "active"` or an unexpired trial, renders an activation screen instead of `children` otherwise — this is a real block, not cosmetic (no `children` render path exists in the non-entitled branch). Independently, the API route's own `checkEntitlement(client, user.id)` (server-side, re-reads `plan_status`/`trial_ends_at` fresh from `profiles`) 402s before any AI call or DB grounding lookup happens — traced this is not reachable-around by a non-Plus user hitting the API directly (e.g. via curl/devtools) even if they never load the page at all. Two independent enforcement points, matches every other AI route in the app.

**404 / not-found handling — traced, no crash, no infinite spinner.** `page.tsx`'s `if (homeworkLoading && !hw) return <Loading…>` then `if (!hw) return <"couldn't be found" + back link>` — a nonexistent or not-owned `homeworkId` resolves to the friendly not-found state once the (cheap, RLS-scoped) `useHomework` fetch completes, never a blank crash. Confirmed the parent `src/app/app/layout.tsx` gates all `/app/*` children (`loading || !user || !profile`) before this page's first render, so `user.id` is always resolved by the time `useHomework(user?.id)` runs — no false-negative "not found" flash during auth loading (same invariant QA already confirmed protects `/app/deadlines`).

**No chat persistence — confirmed true, not just claimed.** Grepped the new route file for `study_tutor_messages`/`.insert(`/`.upsert(` — zero writes anywhere in `route.ts` beyond the single scoped `.select()`. The route is a pure read-then-generate-then-respond function.

**Entry-point rendering — re-rendered myself, not taken on Dev's word.** Built a real-JSX Playwright harness (temporary unauthenticated route, exact `Card`/`CardContent`/`PriorityBadge`/`Sparkles`/`Link` components and classNames copy-pasted verbatim from the merged `StudentSchoolHome.tsx`, deleted before commit — confirmed absent via `git status` and a clean `next build` route list afterward) at 320/375/390px with two deliberately long subject+title combinations ("Advanced Placement Chemistry: Complete the stoichiometry and limiting-reagent worksheet problems 1 through 20", "English Literature and Composition: Write a five-paragraph comparative essay..."). Result: `document.documentElement.scrollWidth === clientWidth` at all 3 widths — **zero page-level horizontal overflow**. The title `<p>` itself reports `scrollWidth > clientWidth` at narrow widths, which is the *correct*, expected behavior of the pre-existing `truncate` class (ellipsis-clip, not a leak) — confirmed visually via screenshot that titles clip cleanly with an ellipsis and the icon button stays fully visible and aligned on every row. The new `<Link aria-label="Get AI help with this homework" title="Get AI help">` renders a consistent 32×32px (`h-8 w-8`) hit target at all 3 widths (measured via `boundingBox()`, not eyeballed) — has both an `aria-label` and a native `title` tooltip, so it's not an unlabeled icon-only control. **Minor, non-blocking note**: 32px is below the commonly-cited 40-44px "comfortable" touch-target guidance (though it clears WCAG 2.5.8 AA's 24×24px minimum), but it's not a regression introduced by this diff — an identical `h-8 w-8` interactive button already exists in this codebase (`src/app/app/school/subjects/[subjectId]/session/page.tsx:393`, the AI-tutor chat's own back/close control) using the exact same size for the exact same "compact icon button next to chat/tutor UI" role. Flagging for a possible future accessibility pass across both sites together, not sending this back to Dev alone — inconsistent to hold one new icon button to a stricter bar than the existing one it was visually modeled on.

**Build gates — run directly, not taken on Dev's report.** This worktree's `HEAD` (`b73ec8a`) did not originally contain Dev's files — my first `tsc`/`build` run silently passed against the *old* code with the new route completely absent from the route list (a process trap worth naming: `git show`/`git diff` can read a commit's blobs without the files being on disk, so a naive "read the diff, then run the existing build" sequence can silently validate the wrong tree). Caught this from the build's route list missing `/api/school/homework-help` and `/app/school/homework/[homeworkId]/help` entirely, corrected by pulling Dev's actual files onto disk, then re-ran both clean: `npx tsc --noEmit` — clean (no output/errors). `npx next build` — clean, **43 routes** including `ƒ /api/school/homework-help` (5.69 kB) and `ƒ /app/school/homework/[homeworkId]/help` (182 kB First Load JS), both dynamic as expected for an auth+DB-backed route/page.

**Consistency spot-checks:** `callStudyAIForText({ system, messages: [{role:"user", content: message}], maxTokens: 900, effort: "low" })` matches the existing `/api/study/tutor` route's call shape exactly (same file, same helper, same maxTokens/effort). `homework.priority` is `not null` with a `check` constraint (`schema.sql:121`) so the system-prompt interpolation can never see an unexpected value. `StudentSchoolHome.tsx`'s diff is a single, isolated hunk (verified via `git show`) — does not touch the nearby `toggleHomework` XP-guard logic fixed in an earlier round, so no regression risk there.

**Verdict: sign-off.** This is a real, adversarial pass — full diff read, DB-scoping traced through `requireUser`→RLS, both paywall layers independently verified (not just read), 404 path traced, persistence claim grepped and confirmed false-negative-free, own Playwright render at 3 real widths with adversarial long-title data (not just a JSX read), own `tsc`/`next build` run against the actual merged code (caught and corrected my own tooling mistake of initially validating the wrong tree). One non-blocking note handed to Cato/Product for a possible future pass: the new 32px icon button and the pre-existing `session/page.tsx:393` icon button it was modeled on are both under the ~40-44px "comfortable" touch-target norm — not a regression, not blocking this feature, worth a consistency pass if/when touch-target sizing gets revisited generally.

## DEV — SIGNUP CONFIRMATION: CODE ENTRY INSTEAD OF EMAIL LINK, worktree `agent-a0eb5d9b08b1f7b53` — 2026-08-31

CEO wanted signup confirmation to work via a typed 6-digit code instead of "click the link" — pure app-side change, `supabase.auth.verifyOtp`/`.resend()` are existing Supabase Auth APIs, no schema/migration touched. Email template + Resend SMTP config handled separately in the Supabase dashboard, not this task's concern.

**Shipped, `src/app/signup/page.tsx` only:**
- Extracted the profile-creation + `/choose-plan` redirect (previously only in the immediate-session branch) into a shared `createProfileAndContinue(userId, fullName)` — still a plain `.insert()`, not `.upsert()` (same PostgREST column-grant restriction as documented for every other `profiles` write in this codebase), called from both the immediate-session path and the new post-verify path.
- Replaced the static "check your email, click the link" screen with a code-entry form: single numeric input (`inputMode="numeric"`, `maxLength={6}`, `autoComplete="one-time-code"`, digits-only filter on change — deliberately not 6 separate boxes, no UX case for the extra complexity here), submits to `supabase.auth.verifyOtp({ email, token, type: "signup" })`. Success (`data.session` present) runs the shared profile-creation function; failure surfaces Supabase's real error message directly (covers both wrong code and expired code — Supabase returns "Token has expired or is invalid" for both, verified via a forced-error harness render, not assumed).
- "Resend code" calls `supabase.auth.resend({ type: "signup", email })` with a 45s client-side cooldown (starts immediately after the initial `signUp()` call too, since that already sent the first email) — disabled button, live countdown via `setInterval`, to protect Resend's send quota per the task's ask.
- Kept the exact pre-existing visual pattern (centered icon+heading+subtext inside the same `<main>`, no `BrandPanel` desktop split) — this was already how the static screen looked, and a short-lived confirmation step isn't the right candidate for the two-column desktop treatment Product added to the primary form views; left as-is rather than inventing new layout, per the task's own default-to-leaving-it framing.
- `src/app/login/page.tsx`: read in full, made no changes. A user who clicks the link instead of entering the code still lands on `/login` via the unchanged `emailRedirectTo`, and `supabase-js`'s `detectSessionInUrl: true` (already configured in `src/lib/supabase/client.ts`) picks up the session from the URL fragment automatically — login's existing first-login profile-creation branch (`!profile` → insert → `/choose-plan`) still applies whether the session came from a password login or a link click, this was already true before my change and I didn't touch it.

**Edge cases handled explicitly:** expired code and wrong code (both surfaced via Supabase's real error message, not swallowed); a user already holding a session landing on this screen (structurally unreachable — `needsConfirmation` only flips true from `handleSubmit`'s no-session branch, and a signed-in user resubmitting the signup form gets Supabase's "already registered" error before that point, never reaching the code screen).

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` and `npx next build` both clean (exit 0, 43 routes, `/signup` at 6.15 kB) — run once with the harness route present (caught the same stale-`.next`-generated-types artifact class documented earlier in this file from a deleted temp route, cleared `.next`, re-ran clean) and once after the harness was fully removed and confirmed absent via `git status`. Rendered the code-entry screen (default, wrong/expired-code-error, and just-resent-with-cooldown states) through a temporary unauthenticated Playwright harness (`/opt/pw-browsers/chromium-1194`, JSX copy-pasted verbatim from the real merged file) at 390px in both themes (theme forced via `localStorage` before nav, since the app's own init script defaults to light when no stored preference exists) — `scrollWidth === clientWidth` in all 6 combinations, zero horizontal overflow, screenshots confirm clean rendering matching the established icon+heading+subtext pattern in both light and dark.

## QA REVIEW — SIGNUP OTP CONFIRMATION (Dev commit `5efd0e73`) — 2026-08-31

Reviewed `git show 5efd0e73d0b3b03065d1efd357db4e540f2328bc` in full (only `PROJECT_STATE.md` + `src/app/signup/page.tsx` touched, +170/-18 in the code file — not sampled). This worktree branched from `e55fe07` before Dev's commit was pushed anywhere, so the merge/cherry-pick tools were unavailable (blocked by the environment's own worktree-isolation guard); brought the file onto disk by writing byte-identical content copied from `git show <commit>:src/app/signup/page.tsx` and confirmed `git diff <commit> -- src/app/signup/page.tsx` was empty before testing, exactly to avoid the "validated the wrong tree" trap this same file's history already flagged once.

**Real bug found — `createProfileAndContinue`'s failure path is unreachable/invisible on the new OTP-verify call site.** The shared helper sets `error` state on a failed `profiles` insert (`setError("Your account was created, but we couldn't set it up yet. Try logging in.")`), which is rendered by the *original* signup-form JSX (`{error && <p className="text-sm text-danger">{error}</p>}` inside the `handleSubmit`-path return). The new `needsConfirmation` (OTP-entry) branch's JSX only renders `{verifyError}` and `{resendMessage}` — it never renders `{error}` anywhere. So: user enters a correct code → `verifyOtp` succeeds (session + user now exist, real Supabase auth session live in the browser) → the `profiles` insert fails for any reason (transient network blip, RLS/grant edge case, etc.) → `setError(...)` fires into a state variable nothing in the currently-rendered tree reads → `verifyLoading` still flips back to `false` so the button re-enables → the user sees **no error message at all**, just the button stop spinning with no explanation, no redirect, stuck on the code-entry screen holding a real but profile-less session.
  - Compounding severity, traced through the rest of the app: if that stuck user then navigates to `/app` (or refreshes and the app's session-restore routes them there), `app/layout.tsx`'s gate is `if (loading || !user || !profile) return <LoadingScreen message="Signing you in…" />` — there is no fallback/redirect when `user` exists but `profile` is permanently `null`; the user hits an infinite "Signing you in…" spinner with zero recovery path from the UI itself. The only actual recovery path is `/login`, whose `handleSubmit` re-queries `profiles`, finds none, and inserts it (confirmed by reading `src/app/login/page.tsx` — untouched by this diff) — but the OTP screen never tells the user to do that in this failure case, and there's no visible signal that anything went wrong to prompt them to try it.
  - This is exactly the "call-site drift" class the task asked me to check for: the two `createProfileAndContinue` call sites are identical in *what they call*, but not identical in *what the surrounding UI does with the result* — the immediate-session path (original form) can show its own failure, the post-verify path (new) silently can't.
  - **Fix needed (for Dev):** either render `{error}` in the `needsConfirmation` branch's JSX too (simplest — same state, just also read it there), or have `createProfileAndContinue` report failure through a shared/passed-in setter so both branches display it. Also worth having `app/layout.tsx` handle a genuinely-`null` profile with a real user by redirecting to `/login` (or `/choose-plan` with a defensive insert) instead of spinning forever — a defensive backstop, not just a symptom fix, since the layout gate itself has no escape hatch for this state regardless of how it's reached.

**Rate limiting / abuse (the substantive question) — the 45s client-side cooldown is real UX, not a real security control, but that's fine here because Supabase's own server-side limits are the actual backstop.** Read the code: `resendCooldown` is plain `useState`, reset to `0` on every fresh mount/page load, and gates nothing except the button's `disabled` prop — `handleResend` itself also checks `resendCooldown > 0` before calling `.resend()`, but that check runs in the browser's own JS, so it protects nothing against a caller that skips the UI (refresh the tab to reset React state, or call `supabase.auth.resend({ type: "signup", email })` directly against the public Supabase REST/GoTrue endpoint with the anon key, which is exposed client-side by design). Confirmed via `WebSearch` against Supabase's own docs (not assumed): Supabase Auth enforces its own **server-side** rate limits independent of any app code — a per-IP token-bucket limiter across auth endpoints (burst capacity ~30), a per-identity OTP-request cooldown of 60s (a fresh `resend`/`signInWithOtp` for the same email is rejected server-side inside that window regardless of what the client sends), and a separate email-sending-quota limit (2/hr on Supabase's default SMTP, 30/hr once a custom SMTP/Resend provider is configured — which PROJECT_STATE.md's own account of this task says is being set up separately). **Verdict: the 45s client cooldown is cosmetic/UX-only and is trivially bypassable exactly as suspected (page refresh, or a direct API call) — but this is not a live vulnerability, because Supabase's real, server-side, per-email/per-IP rate limiting is what actually protects the Resend sending quota, and that layer is independent of anything this diff does or doesn't do.** Not blocking. Worth noting to Cato as a documentation/expectation-setting item only: don't let "we added a 45s cooldown" get repeated later as if it were the actual rate-limit control — it isn't, Supabase's server-side config is, and that's a dashboard setting outside this diff's scope (consistent with the task's own framing that SMTP/email config is being handled separately).

**Code/token handling — clean.** Confirmed via a live interactive Playwright test (not just reading the regex): typed `"12ab34#$56789"` into the real running code input and the DOM value after typing was `"123456"` — the `.replace(/[^0-9]/g, "").slice(0, 6)` filter genuinely strips non-digits and caps length, matching Dev's claim. Grepped the whole file for `console.log`/`localStorage`/any second consumer of the `code` state — the only place `code` (as `trimmed`) is used is the single `verifyOtp({ email, token: trimmed, type: "signup" })` call; it is never logged, never persisted (no `localStorage`/`sessionStorage`/cookie write anywhere in the file), and never sent anywhere else.

**Error message honesty — confirmed, not taken on Dev's word.** Read `handleVerifyCode`: on `verifyErr` it does `setVerifyError(verifyErr.message)` directly — Supabase's real error text is surfaced verbatim for both wrong-code and expired-code cases (both produce Supabase's own message, e.g. "Token has expired or is invalid" per Supabase's documented behavior for `verifyOtp`), nothing swallowed or genericized. Same pattern in `handleResend` (`setVerifyError(resendErr.message)`). The only *invented* copy in the whole flow is the generic fallback `"Couldn't verify that code. Try again or request a new one."`, used only in the narrow case where `verifyOtp` returns no error but also no `session`/`user` — a reasonable, honestly-labeled fallback for a genuinely edge case, not a cover for a real error being hidden.

**`createProfileAndContinue` — confirmed plain `.insert()`, never `.upsert()`,** both call sites use the identical function/call shape (`createProfileAndContinue(userId, fullName)`) with no drift in *what* is written — the only difference is the `fullName` argument's fallback chain in the post-verify path (`name.trim() || (data.user.email?.split("@")[0] ?? "Student")` vs. the immediate-session path's plain `name.trim()`), which is a strict superset of safety (defends against `name` being empty if the component ever remounted between initial submit and verify) and not a regression. The *drift that does matter* is the error-rendering gap documented above — the call itself is identical, the surrounding UI isn't.

**`/login` "pre-existing" claim — verified accurate, not taken on Dev's word.** `git show 5efd0e73... --stat` confirms only `PROJECT_STATE.md` and `src/app/signup/page.tsx` were touched by this commit; `src/app/login/page.tsx` is untouched. Read `login/page.tsx` in full: `handleSubmit` is genuinely the only place profile-creation runs (queries `profiles`, inserts if absent, then routes) — a user who lands there via the `detectSessionInUrl` link-click fallback (already-authenticated, `/login`'s form never auto-submits) does have to actually submit the login form before a profile row is created for them. Confirmed pre-existing and correctly out of scope for this diff, not touched or worsened by it.

**Render check — own render, not Dev's screenshots (Dev's harness was gone before this commit, as expected).** Started the dev server directly (`npx next dev`), temporarily forced `needsConfirmation`'s initial `useState` to `true` (plus a placeholder `email` value) purely to reach the branch without a live Supabase session, screenshotted via Playwright/Chromium at 390px in both themes (theme forced via `localStorage.setItem('alxioum_theme', ...)` before navigation, matching the app's own init-script convention), then reverted both temporary edits and re-confirmed `git diff 5efd0e73... -- src/app/signup/page.tsx` was empty again before running the build gates. Both renders are clean: code input, "Confirm account" button, "Resend code" link, and the "prefer the link" fallback copy all render correctly with no overflow/wrap issues in dark and light, `:focus-visible` ring shows correctly on the autofocused input. No hydration/console errors in the dev-server log for either load (only pre-existing infra noise: a benign multi-lockfile workspace-root warning, unrelated to this diff).

**Build gates — run directly.** `npx tsc --noEmit`: clean (exit 0). `npx next build`: clean (exit 0), all 43 routes including `/signup` at 6.15 kB.

**Verdict: NOT signed off as-is — one real bug (silent-failure UI gap on `createProfileAndContinue`'s error path in the new OTP-verify branch) needs a fix before this ships.** Everything else held up under adversarial testing: digits-only filtering (verified interactively), error-message honesty (verified by reading the actual code path, not Dev's description), no `.upsert()`/no call-site drift in the write itself, the `/login` pre-existing-behavior claim (verified via `git show --stat` + full file read), and the resend-cooldown rate-limiting question (verified against Supabase's own documented server-side limits, not assumed) — the 45s client cooldown is real but purely cosmetic, and that's fine because Supabase's server-side rate limiting is the actual, independent backstop. Sending back to Dev for: (1) render `{error}` in the `needsConfirmation` branch (or route both call sites through one shared error-display setter), (2) consider a defensive fallback in `app/layout.tsx` for the `user`-without-`profile` state instead of an unrecoverable infinite spinner. Will retest both once fixed.

BLOCKERS: Bug above blocks sign-off — not deployable as-is (silent failure with no recovery path in a real, if narrow, failure case).

NEXT TASK: Dev fixes the two items above; QA retests the `needsConfirmation` branch's error rendering (forced-failure case) and the `app/layout.tsx` defensive fallback, then hands to Cato.

## DEV — FIX: SIGNUP OTP SILENT PROFILE-CREATION FAILURE, worktree `agent-a542a83380afadd79` — 2026-08-31

Fixed the bug QA sent back (`createProfileAndContinue` failure after a successful `verifyOtp` was invisible — session live, no profile row, no error shown, no recovery). This worktree branched from `e55fe07`, before Dev's OTP commit (`5efd0e73`) had been pushed anywhere, so cherry-picked QA's own commit `a9fb383` (which already carries `5efd0e73`'s code byte-identical, per QA's own note, plus their review) directly onto this branch — clean cherry-pick, confirmed via `git diff` before editing.

**`src/app/signup/page.tsx`:**
- Added `verifiedUserId` state, set the moment `verifyOtp` succeeds (real session/user exist from that point on). Distinguishes "still entering the code" from "code was correct, the follow-up `profiles` insert failed" — the latter is not a code-verification failure and re-submitting the same (now-consumed) code would just produce a confusing "already used" error, so it gets its own branch instead of falling through to the code-entry form.
- New recovery view, rendered when `verifiedUserId && error`: explains the account is confirmed but setup didn't finish, shows the real `error` message (previously swallowed), and offers two ways forward — "Try again" (calls new `handleRetryProfile()`, which re-runs just the `profiles` insert against the existing session, no code re-entry needed) and "Log in instead" (links to `/login`, whose `handleSubmit` already re-queries/inserts a missing profile — confirmed working, not new).
- Normal code-entry view: `{(verifyError || error) && ...}` now reads `error` too (previously only `verifyError`/`resendMessage`), so the shared error state is never silently dropped regardless of which path set it. In practice `error` is only ever truthy alongside `verifiedUserId`, so this mainly serves as the documented shared-display QA suggested — the dedicated recovery view is what actually fires for this bug.
- `handleVerifyCode` now clears `error` (not just `verifyError`) at the start of each attempt, for the same hygiene reason `handleSubmit` already clears `error` at the start of its own attempts.

**`src/app/app/layout.tsx`:** added a fallback for `user` truthy + `profile` genuinely `null` (not just still loading). `AuthProvider`'s initial `loading` flip to `false` only happens after its `loadProfile` call is awaited (read `AuthProvider.tsx` in full to confirm this, not assumed), so by the time `loading` is `false` in this gate, a missing `profile` is real, not a race — there's no code path that would make one appear later on its own. Split the old single `if (loading || !user || !profile)` gate into `if (loading || !user)` (spinner, unchanged) and a new `if (!profile)` branch showing a real message ("We couldn't find your profile… Logging back in usually fixes this.") with a "Go to log in" button — same visual language as the new signup recovery view (danger-tinted icon badge, heading, subtext, single CTA).

**Reproduced the bug live before fixing it, not assumed from QA's report:** since this sandbox has no real Supabase network access (confirmed again this session — `curl` to the project's `*.supabase.co` host returns a 403 tunnel failure, same org egress block prior QA passes hit), set a temporary local-only `.env.local` (gitignored, deleted before commit) pointing `NEXT_PUBLIC_SUPABASE_URL` at a fake host, ran the real dev server, and used Playwright's network-route interception to mock exactly the 3 calls this flow makes: `POST /auth/v1/signup` (no session → code-entry screen), `POST /auth/v1/verify` (success, real session), `POST /rest/v1/profiles` (mocked `409` to simulate a real insert failure). On the pre-fix code (`git stash` to the QA-reviewed commit), typing the correct code and submitting reproduced exactly what QA described: the button re-enabled, code input still showed the entered digits, and the rendered DOM had zero error text anywhere — confirmed via `page.locator("main").innerText()` and a screenshot. Restored the fix (`git stash pop`) and re-ran the identical script: the recovery view now renders with the real error message and both buttons. Then verified the retry path end-to-end with a second Playwright run (profile insert mocked to fail once, then succeed) — "Try again" correctly calls just the profile insert (no re-verify), and on success navigates to `/choose-plan`.

**Regression-checked the rest of the OTP flow with the same interception technique**, all passing: correct code still verifies and navigates to `/choose-plan` on the first try when the profile insert succeeds; wrong/expired code still shows Supabase's real error inline with no navigation; resend cooldown still starts at 45s and the button is still disabled during it.

**`app/layout.tsx` fallback verified separately:** seeded a fake, valid Supabase session directly into `localStorage` (matching the real `sb-<project-ref>-auth-token` key `supabase-js` uses) so `getSession()` resolves with a user with no network call, mocked the `profiles` GET to return `[]` (genuinely-absent row, not a timing artifact), and loaded `/app` directly. The new fallback renders correctly in both themes at 390px — confirmed via screenshot, no layout/overflow issues, matches the signup recovery view's visual language.

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean (exit 0). `npx next build` clean (exit 0, all 43 routes). `.env.local` and all scratch Playwright scripts/screenshots were outside the repo or deleted before commit — `git status` confirms only the two intended source files changed.

BLOCKERS: None.

NEXT TASK: Handing back to QA to re-verify the fix (forced-failure recovery view, retry path, `app/layout.tsx` fallback) before Cato/CEO merge and deploy.

DEPLOYMENT STATUS: not deployed — committed locally on `claude/futureos-student-app-3ewdz6` via worktree `agent-a542a83380afadd79`, not pushed, per instruction (QA re-verifies before merge/deploy).

## QA RE-REVIEW — SIGNUP OTP FIX (Dev commit `91e94bb`) — 2026-08-31

Second pass on this feature. Adversarial, not a rubber stamp — independently reproduced everything rather than trusting Dev's report, and found one new real regression the fix itself introduces.

**Lineage verified as real, not just claimed.** This worktree started at `e55fe07` (no OTP code at all). Cherry-picked `3b94a79` (my own prior review commit, which QA's own note confirms is content-identical to `a9fb383` — verified again here via `git diff a9fb383 3b94a79` returning empty) — clean, no conflicts. Then cherry-picked `91e94bb` (Dev's fix) directly on top — also clean, no conflicts. `git diff 91e94bb HEAD -- src/app/signup/page.tsx src/app/app/layout.tsx` is empty: this worktree's HEAD (`f0d0011`) carries byte-identical content to `91e94bb`. The claimed history (fix built on top of my sign-off commit, which carries the OTP feature) is genuine, confirmed by content diff, not commit-message trust.

**Read `git show 91e94bb` in full (3 files, +113/-4) — not sampled.** Matches Dev's description: `verifiedUserId` state set on successful `verifyOtp`; new recovery view gated on `verifiedUserId && error`; `error` now also read in the normal code-entry view's error display; `handleVerifyCode` clears `error` at the start of each attempt; `app/layout.tsx` split into `if (loading || !user)` (unchanged spinner) + new `if (!profile)` fallback (real message + "Go to log in").

**Independent repro, built from scratch (not Dev's harness).** No live Supabase access in this sandbox either (same org egress block, unrelated to this diff). Applied the actual committed `91e94bb` file content into a real `npx next dev` server via a temporary `.env.local` pointing at a fake Supabase host, and used Playwright network-route interception (own script, not Dev's) to mock `auth/v1/signup`, `auth/v1/verify`, and `rest/v1/profiles`:
- Forced a `409` on the first `profiles` POST after a successful `verifyOtp` (real session established, matching mocked JWT/session shape) — the recovery view rendered with the exact real error text (`"Your account was created, but we couldn't set it up yet. Try logging in."`), both buttons present. Confirmed this is the genuine `createProfileAndContinue` failure path, not a stub.
- Clicked "Try again" — confirmed via network log it fires only a second `POST /rest/v1/profiles` (no second `POST /auth/v1/verify` — the already-consumed code is never resubmitted), and on that second call succeeding, navigated cleanly to `/choose-plan`. `handleRetryProfile` does exactly what it claims: retries only the profile insert against the live session.
- Regression suite (own script, 3 independent scenarios): correct code + successful insert on the first try → `/choose-plan` (PASS). Wrong/expired code → Supabase's real error text rendered inline, no navigation (PASS). Resend cooldown → button disabled, reads `"Resend code (45s)"` immediately after the first send (PASS). All three hold, unchanged from before this fix.
- Render check, both new UI states, 390px, both themes (own Playwright harness, `localStorage`-forced theme matching the app's own init-script convention): signup recovery view and `app/layout.tsx`'s `!profile` fallback both render cleanly — `document.documentElement.scrollWidth === clientWidth` true in all 4 combinations (recovery×light, recovery×dark, fallback×light, fallback×dark), no truncation/overflow, danger-tinted icon badge + heading + subtext + CTA(s) all visible and correctly styled. Screenshots taken and reviewed directly, not assumed from a JSX read.
- Noted, not a bug: a pre-existing client-hydration warning (`<html>` `className` gains `light` after mount) shows on every page load in this dev-mode harness — traced to `src/components/providers/ThemeProvider.tsx`'s deliberate pre-hydration inline theme script (`themeInitScript`, documented in its own code comment as intentional flash-prevention), confirmed unrelated to this diff and pre-existing app-wide.

**Item 4 (the "does `loading=false` guarantee `loadProfile` resolved" claim) — Dev's claim is FALSE for the case that actually matters, and this is a real, reproducible regression.** Read `AuthProvider.tsx` in full. Dev's claim is correct only for the *initial* mount path: the first-load effect does `getSession().then(...).finally(() => setLoading(false))`, so `loading` genuinely gates the first profile fetch. But `AuthProvider` also has a second, independent path — `supabase.auth.onAuthStateChange((_event, session) => { setUser(...); if (session?.user) loadProfile(session.user.id); ... })` — and this handler **never touches `loading` at all**. Every subsequent sign-in event (a normal login, an OTP verify, an OAuth callback, anything that fires `onAuthStateChange` after the app has already resolved its initial "no session" state with `loading=false`) sets `user` truthy immediately and kicks off `loadProfile` *unawaited*, while `loading` stays permanently `false` from the initial mount. This is a real, pre-existing gap in `AuthProvider`'s design — invisible before this diff because the old `app/layout.tsx` gate (`loading || !user || !profile`) just kept showing the soft, self-correcting `"Signing you in…"` spinner during that window. Dev's new `!profile` branch replaces that spinner with a hard, dead-end-looking `"We couldn't find your profile… Go to log in"` screen — and that screen can now flash for a **completely normal, fully-onboarded returning user on an ordinary login**, not just the narrow silent-failure case this fix was built for.
  - **Reproduced end-to-end, own script**, mocking `/login`'s real flow: `signInWithPassword` succeeds → `onAuthStateChange` fires (`AuthProvider`'s own `loadProfile`, a `select=*` GET, kicked off unawaited) → `login/page.tsx`'s own *separate*, independent `.select("onboarding_completed, billing_interval")` query resolves (a different GET, same table) → `router.push("/app")` fires *before* `AuthProvider`'s own GET has resolved. Added realistic latency (500ms) to `AuthProvider`'s GET only, matching its actual request start time (logged, ~1ms apart from the login page's own query) — confirmed via timestamped logs that both requests start almost simultaneously, so any real-world scenario where `AuthProvider`'s request happens to take even a few hundred ms longer than the login page's own request (plausible: HTTP/2 connection contention, mobile network jitter, server-side queuing — not a contrived edge case) reproduces this. At 50ms after navigating to `/app`, the rendered page is the new `"We couldn't find your profile"` fallback screen — for a user whose account and profile are completely fine and fully onboarded. ~600ms later, once `AuthProvider`'s own fetch resolves, the page silently flips to the real dashboard. Screenshotted (both the false-positive state and the corrected state) — this is not a theoretical race, it's directly observable and reproduced multiple times at realistic delay values (500ms; also reproduced once at 400ms, did not reproduce at 120–250ms in this specific harness's timing, consistent with it being a genuine, delay-magnitude-dependent race rather than either "always broken" or "impossible").
  - **Severity:** lower than the original bug (that one had zero recovery path, ever; this one self-corrects within roughly a second once the lagging fetch resolves) — but it is a real, user-visible regression on the single most common code path in the app (every normal login), and if a user acts on it before it self-corrects (clicks "Go to log in" during the flash window), it actively logs out/redirects a fully-working account for no reason, which is worse than the soft spinner it replaced.
  - **Fix needed (for Dev):** the `!profile` fallback needs to distinguish "genuinely resolved, no profile row" from "a profile fetch is in flight for a just-authenticated user." The cleanest fix is in `AuthProvider.tsx` itself, not `app/layout.tsx`: make the `onAuthStateChange` handler track in-flight profile loads the same way the initial-mount effect already does (e.g., a `profileLoading` flag set before calling `loadProfile` and cleared after, exposed via the context, with `app/layout.tsx` gating on `loading || profileLoading` before evaluating `!profile`) — but this needs care to *not* re-trigger a full loading gate on routine `TOKEN_REFRESHED` events for an already-rendering, already-profiled user (which would introduce a new flicker regression of its own). Flagging the design constraint, not prescribing the exact implementation — that's Dev's call.

**Verdict: NOT signed off.** The original silent-failure bug (this task's starting point) is genuinely fixed — recovery view, retry path, and regression suite all verified independently and hold up. But the fix introduces a new, real, independently-reproduced regression: a false-positive "profile not found, log back in" screen that can flash for ordinary, fully-working returning users on a normal login, caused by a pre-existing gap in `AuthProvider`'s `onAuthStateChange` handler that Dev's own stated reasoning ("`loading` being false guarantees `loadProfile` resolved") does not actually hold for. Sending back to Dev for the `AuthProvider` race fix described above; will re-verify with the same login-race repro script once fixed, plus the previously-passing recovery-view/retry/regression suite to confirm no new regression from whatever fix lands.

BLOCKERS: Bug above blocks sign-off — a real, reproduced regression on the ordinary login path, not deployable as-is.

NEXT TASK: Dev fixes the `AuthProvider` onAuthStateChange/loading-state gap described above (design constraint: must not introduce a new loading-flicker regression on routine token-refresh events for already-authenticated users). QA re-verifies via the same login-race Playwright repro (mocked `signInWithPassword` + delayed `AuthProvider` profile fetch) plus re-confirms the recovery view/retry path/regression suite still hold, then hands to Cato.

## DEV — FIX: AUTHPROVIDER onAuthStateChange PROFILE-LOAD RACE (QA regression on `00bcb5d`) — 2026-08-31

Fixed the regression QA sent back: `AuthProvider`'s `onAuthStateChange` handler kicks off an unawaited `loadProfile` on every auth event (not just the initial mount) without ever touching `loading`, so a fresh sign-in racing `login/page.tsx`'s own separate profile query could land on `app/layout.tsx`'s new `!profile` fallback for a completely healthy, fully-onboarded account for a few hundred ms.

**Worktree note:** this worktree's branch (`worktree-agent-adf465bc9bd0347aa`) had not yet received the OTP-signup feature or either round of the fix/QA-regression commits (`5efd0e73` → `3b94a79`/`3b1f11b` → `91e94bb`/`f0d0011` → `00bcb5d`) — confirmed via `git log`/`git branch --all --contains`. That whole chain lives on `worktree-agent-aa76c0857f143361b` and is a straight linear descendant of this worktree's own prior HEAD (`e55fe07`), so fast-forward-merged `00bcb5d` in (`git merge --ff-only`, no conflicts, no rebase) to get onto the exact code QA reviewed before making the fix on top.

**`src/components/providers/AuthProvider.tsx`:**
- Added `profileLoading` state, exposed on the context alongside `loading`/`user`/`profile`. True only while a profile fetch for a *newly-seen* user id is in flight (a fresh sign-in, or a user switch) — deliberately not set for repeat events on an already-known user id.
- Added a `lastUserIdRef` (a `useRef<string | null>`) tracking the user id the provider has already started/finished loading a profile for. The initial-mount `getSession()` effect now sets this ref alongside its existing `loading`-gated load. `onAuthStateChange`'s handler now branches on `nextUserId !== lastUserIdRef.current`:
  - **Different/new user id** (a real sign-in event, or a user switch): this is the case the previous fix's `loading` check missed entirely. Sets `profileLoading(true)`, updates the ref, calls `loadProfile(nextUserId).finally(() => setProfileLoading(false))`.
  - **Same user id as already loaded** (routine `TOKEN_REFRESHED`, `USER_UPDATED`, a duplicate `INITIAL_SESSION` fire racing the mount effect, etc.): still calls `loadProfile` in the background to stay fresh (preserves the pre-existing "refresh on any auth event" behavior), but never touches `profileLoading` — so no already-rendering, already-authenticated screen can flicker from this branch, regardless of how slow that background fetch is.
  - No session (sign-out): resets the ref to `null` and `profileLoading` to `false` alongside the existing `setProfile(null)`.

**`src/app/app/layout.tsx`:** gate changed from `if (loading || !user)` to `if (loading || !user || profileLoading)` before falling through to the `!profile` genuine-failure fallback. Updated the stale comment (previously claimed `loading` alone was sufficient — QA's finding disproved that for the `onAuthStateChange` path) to explain both `loading` (initial mount) and `profileLoading` (every subsequent auth event) are what jointly guarantee a `null` profile reaching that branch is real, not in-flight.

**Reproduced QA's exact race first, confirmed it's gone, confirmed no new flicker — all via Playwright network-interception against a real `npx next dev` server** (no live Supabase access in this sandbox, confirmed blocked again this session; temporary gitignored `.env.local` pointing at a fake host, deleted before commit):
1. **Repro against pre-fix code first (`git stash` back to `00bcb5d`'s `AuthProvider.tsx`/`layout.tsx`):** re-ran QA's own repro script (mocked `signInWithPassword` fast, `AuthProvider`'s `select=*` profiles GET delayed 500ms, `login/page.tsx`'s own separate query fast) — confirmed the bug reproduces: at 50ms after navigating to `/app`, the rendered body is exactly `"We couldn't find your profile… Go to log in"` for a fully healthy, onboarded account; ~600ms later it self-corrects to the real dashboard. This confirms the repro script is valid before trusting any "fixed" result from it.
2. **Restored the fix (`git stash pop`), re-ran the identical script:** at 50ms after navigating to `/app`, the rendered body is now the `"Signing you in…"` loading screen (not the dead-end fallback), then settles into the real dashboard once the fetch resolves. Fallback text never appears at any sampled point.
3. **Stress test at a wider delay:** same repro with the `AuthProvider` profile GET artificially delayed 2000ms (far exceeding the original 500ms) and the render polled every 100ms for 2.5s post-navigation — fallback text never appears at any sample; final state is the real dashboard.
4. **Token-refresh flicker test (the explicit QA constraint), own script:** logged in normally, let `/app` fully settle on real content, then fired a **real** `TOKEN_REFRESHED` event through the page's own live Supabase client (`supabase.auth.refreshSession()` — the same public API the SDK's own internal refresh timer calls, same user id, no navigation) while sampling the DOM every 25ms for ~1s. Zero samples showed the loading screen or the fallback screen across 40 samples — confirms the "same user id" branch never touches `profileLoading`. Re-ran with the background profile refetch (the one still fired for staleness) artificially delayed 1500ms and sampled every 25ms for 2.2s (87 samples) — still zero flicker, proving the no-flicker guarantee holds regardless of that background call's latency, not just when it happens to be fast. (The Supabase client instance was temporarily exposed via a dev-only `window` hook in `client.ts` to call `refreshSession()` from Playwright; removed before commit — confirmed via `git status`/`git diff` showing `client.ts` byte-identical to `HEAD` afterward.)
5. **Full regression suite re-run** (own script, same 4 scenarios QA validated on `91e94bb`/`00bcb5d`, all against the current fixed code): correct code + successful insert on first try → `/choose-plan` (PASS). Wrong/expired code → Supabase's real error text inline, no navigation (PASS). Resend cooldown → button disabled, reads `"Resend code (45s)"` immediately after signup (PASS). Genuine profile-creation failure (forced `409` on first insert after a real `verifyOtp` success) → recovery view renders with the real error text, "Log in instead" link present and pointing to `/login`, "Try again" retries only the insert (no re-verify) and succeeds on the second attempt, navigating to `/choose-plan` (PASS). All 4 hold, unchanged by this fix.

**Verification:** `npx tsc --noEmit` clean (exit 0). `npx next build` clean (exit 0, all 52 routes). `git status` confirms only `src/app/app/layout.tsx` and `src/components/providers/AuthProvider.tsx` changed — the temporary `.env.local` and the dev-only `client.ts` test hook were both removed before this check, and all scratch Playwright scripts/screenshots live outside the repo (session scratchpad).

BLOCKERS: None.

NEXT TASK: Handing back to QA to re-verify: the original login-race repro (should no longer flash the fallback at any delay), a dedicated token-refresh-flicker check (should show zero loading/fallback flicker), and a re-confirmation of the full regression suite (OTP success, wrong/expired code, resend cooldown, genuine-failure recovery view + Try again + Log in instead) — before Cato/CEO merge and deploy.

DEPLOYMENT STATUS: not deployed — committed locally on `claude/futureos-student-app-3ewdz6` (worktree `worktree-agent-adf465bc9bd0347aa`), not pushed, per instruction (QA re-verifies before merge/deploy).

DEPLOYMENT STATUS (this QA pass): not deployed — reviewed and committed locally on `claude/futureos-student-app-3ewdz6` via worktree `agent-aa76c0857f143361b`, not pushed. Do not merge/deploy — a real regression was found this pass.
