## QA ROUND 3 — SIGNUP OTP, `AuthProvider` RACE FIX (Dev commit `f2612a6`) — 2026-08-31

Third round on this feature. This worktree (`agent-a82e19c284bd68767`) started at `e55fe07` and had none of the OTP chain on disk — fast-forward-merged `worktree-agent-adf465bc9bd0347aa` (`f2612a6`) in cleanly (`git merge --ff-only`, no conflicts) to get the full lineage before reviewing, confirmed via `git log --oneline` showing the complete chain (`5efd0e73`→`3b94a79`/`3b1f11b`→`91e94bb`/`f0d0011`→`00bcb5d`→`f2612a6`) and a direct read of `src/app/signup/page.tsx` confirming fix 1's recovery view (`verifiedUserId && error` branch, "Try again"/"Log in instead") is genuinely still present alongside fix 2's `AuthProvider`/`app/layout.tsx` changes — lineage is real, not just claimed.

**1. Diff read in full.** `git show f2612a6` — `AuthProvider.tsx` (+57/-4 net vs. pre-round-2-fix) and `app/layout.tsx` (+33/-16-ish) matches Dev's description exactly: `profileLoading` state + `lastUserIdRef`, branching `onAuthStateChange` on `nextUserId !== lastUserIdRef.current` (new/different user → `profileLoading(true)` + tracked load; same user → background-only refresh, `profileLoading` untouched; no session → resets both `lastUserIdRef` and `profileLoading`). `app/layout.tsx` gate changed to `loading || !user || profileLoading` before the `!profile` fallback.

**2. My own login-race repro, built from scratch** (own Playwright script, `qa1_login_race.mjs` — did not reuse Dev's harness). Mocked `/auth/v1/token`, `/auth/v1/logout`, `/rest/v1/profiles` via `page.route`, drove the real `/login` form, delayed the mocked profile GET by 500ms and 2000ms. Result: **0/36 and 0/108 samples showed the fallback screen** — confirmed the fallback genuinely never appears for a healthy sign-in regardless of the profile-fetch delay tested. Matches Dev's claim.

**3. Token-refresh-flicker check, own script, using a REAL `TOKEN_REFRESHED` event** (`qa2_token_refresh_flicker.mjs`) — this was the explicit constraint I set in round 2's report, and I verified Dev actually met it rather than trusting the claim. Seeded a session with `expires_at` ~3s out so supabase-js's own internal auto-refresh timer fired a genuine `POST /auth/v1/token?grant_type=refresh_token` on its own (confirmed via `refreshCount: 1` in both runs — not simulated). Sampled the DOM every 25ms across the refresh, at both 0ms and 1500ms artificial delay on the background same-user profile refetch. Result: **0 fallback hits, 0 spinner hits across 196 and 250 samples respectively.** Zero flicker, independently confirmed.

**4. `lastUserIdRef` edge cases — found a real, unreported bug here.** Traced the actual ref-comparison logic rather than assuming it's correct because the two tested scenarios passed:
- **Plain sign-out (no re-login):** `!nextUserId` branch resets `lastUserIdRef.current = null` and `setProfileLoading(false)` unconditionally — not stuck `true` forever. Confirmed by code read.
- **Sign out, sign back in as a genuinely different user:** the `nextUserId !== lastUserIdRef.current` check correctly treats the second sign-in as newly-seen (ref was reset to `null` on sign-out) and sets `profileLoading(true)` again. So far so good.
- **The bug: `loadProfile()` has no staleness guard, and this is exploitable exactly in the sign-out→different-user scenario the task asked me to check.** Built a dedicated repro (`qa3_signout_switch_user_race.mjs`) using a temporary debug route (`src/app/qa-debug-auth-xyz789/page.tsx`, deleted before commit — confirmed via `git status`/`git diff` clean afterward) that dumps raw `useAuth()` context state, plus a temporary `window.__qaSupabase` hook in `client.ts` (same technique Dev used in its own verification, also fully reverted before commit — confirmed via `git diff src/lib/supabase/client.ts` empty). Sequence: sign in as User A (profile fetch mocked at 4000ms delay) → wait 150ms (fetch still in flight) → sign out → immediately sign in as User B (profile fetch mocked at 100ms delay, resolves fast and correctly). **Result: at t+5102ms, `userId=uid-userb` but `profileId=uid-usera`, `profileFullName=Alpha Anderson` — User A's stale, late-resolving profile fetch overwrote User B's already-correctly-loaded profile in `AuthContext`, 34/~130 samples.** Root cause: the "different user" branch's `loadProfile(nextUserId).finally(() => setProfileLoading(false))` — and `loadProfile` itself — has no check that `nextUserId` is still the *current* user by the time the async response arrives. Two overlapping loads for two different users race, and whichever resolves last wins, regardless of which one is actually current.
  - **Severity:** real, not theoretical — reproduced cleanly and repeatably. `Profile` (`src/lib/types.ts`) carries `plan`, `plan_status`, `trial_ends_at`, `track`, `xp_*`, `full_name`, `avatar_emoji`, `year_group`, `country`. Checked the blast radius: `PaywallGate.tsx` gates purely on the client `profile` object (`isEntitled(profile)`), so a stale profile from a different (e.g. Plus) account could incorrectly unlock/lock the paywall UI for the live user. This is **UI-state only, not a server-side privilege escalation** — confirmed in an earlier round that `checkEntitlement` on the API side re-reads `profiles` fresh from the DB using the caller's real JWT-derived `user.id`, independent of the client's cached context object, so this can't be used to actually consume gated AI features. But it is a real, confirmed cross-account data leak in client state: on a shared/public device, or any fast account-switch flow, User B's session can end up displaying User A's name/avatar/plan/track under a live, correctly-authenticated User B session. That's a privacy/product-correctness bug regardless of the entitlement backstop, and it directly fits the scenario the task named as the highest-risk untested edge case.
  - **Fix needed (for Dev):** `loadProfile`'s `setProfile(data)` write (and the "different user" branch's `setProfileLoading(false)`) needs to check it's still the load that matters before writing — e.g. compare against `lastUserIdRef.current` (or a monotonic request-generation counter) at resolve time and no-op if it's stale: `loadProfile(userId).then(data => { if (lastUserIdRef.current === userId) { setProfile(data); } })`, and same guard on the `finally` that flips `profileLoading`. This needs to preserve the existing "same user, background refresh" behavior (still safe to write there since the id hasn't changed) — only the cross-user overlap needs guarding.
  - Verified this is **not** a confounder in Tests 2/3 above: the healthy-login and token-refresh tests only ever involve one user id at a time, so there's no ordering ambiguity to expose this bug there — it's specifically the two-different-overlapping-users case that's broken, which is why it survived two full regression passes without being caught until this round's dedicated edge-case testing.

**5. Genuine `!profile` fallback (real failure, not a race) — confirmed still works, not overcorrected into permanent hiding.** Own script (`qa4_genuine_no_profile.mjs`): seeded a session for a user whose mocked `profiles` GET returns `[]` with **zero artificial delay** (a real, completed, empty response — not a timing race). Result: fallback text appeared at t=195ms and stayed, in 70/72 samples; never stuck spinning. The `profileLoading` fix does not mask a genuine failure — it correctly resolves to `false` once the (empty) response lands, and `!profile` fires as intended.

**6. Route count — investigated, resolved as a reporting-consistency issue, not a routing bug.** Dev's "52 routes" claim and my own fresh `npx next build` ("53 routes," see below) are both well above the "42-43" figure repeated in earlier rounds' `PROJECT_STATE.md` entries. Cross-checked by counting actual `page.tsx`/`route.ts` files on disk at each historical commit via `git ls-tree`: `b73ec8a` (reported "42 routes") → **49 real files**; `e55fe07` (reported "43 routes") → **51 real files**; `f2612a6`/this round → **51 real files, unchanged from `e55fe07`** (expected — the OTP diff touches only `signup/page.tsx`, `app/layout.tsx`, `AuthProvider.tsx`, zero new route files, confirmed via `git diff e55fe07 f2612a6 --stat`). My own build's route list (53 lines) was cross-referenced 1:1 against every real `page.tsx`/`route.ts` file on disk (51) plus the two synthetic entries every Next.js build always includes (`/_not-found`, `/icon.png`) — **53 = 51 + 2 exactly, zero unexplained, duplicate, or stale/harness entries.** My temporary `qa-debug-auth-xyz789` debug route was deleted before this build ran (confirmed absent from the route list and via `git status`). Conclusion: the historical "42/43" figures under-reported the true file count by 7-8 at the time they were written (likely earlier agents excluding `/icon.png`/`/_not-found` inconsistently, or simply miscounting) — this is a **reporting-consistency problem across rounds, not a route-explosion bug introduced by this diff.** Recommend future rounds count via `find src/app -name "page.tsx" -o -name "route.ts" | wc -l` (unambiguous) rather than eyeballing the build's printed tree. Dev's own "52" vs. my measured "53" is a 1-route discrepancy of the same harmless-accounting-convention flavor, not investigated further since the file-level reconciliation already accounts for the real total exactly.

**7. Full regression suite, own script, against the real `/signup` page (`qa5_full_regression.mjs`)** — driven through the actual form (fill/click), not the SDK directly:
   - Correct code + successful profile insert → `/choose-plan` — **PASS**.
   - Wrong/expired code → Supabase's real error text (`"Token has expired or is invalid"`) shown inline, still on the code-entry screen, no navigation — **PASS**.
   - Resend cooldown → button reads `"Resend code (45s)"` and is disabled immediately after signup — **PASS**.
   - Genuine profile-insert failure (mocked `409` on first insert) → recovery view (`"Almost there"`) renders with the real error text, `"Log in instead"` link present pointing to `/login`, `"Try again"` retries only the insert (2nd attempt succeeds) and navigates to `/choose-plan` — **PASS**, all sub-checks.

**8. Build gates, run directly.** `npx tsc --noEmit`: clean (exit 0, no output). `npx next build`: clean (exit 0), 53 routes (51 real `page.tsx`/`route.ts` files + `/_not-found` + `/icon.png`, reconciled exactly per item 6). Both run after all temporary QA instrumentation (`.env.local`, the debug route, the `client.ts` window hook) was fully removed — confirmed via `git status` showing a clean tree before each gate run.

**9. Lineage — confirmed genuine**, see intro above: full commit chain present, fix 1's recovery view still byte-present in the merged `signup/page.tsx`, not silently dropped by fix 2.

**Verdict: NOT signed off.** Everything Dev's report claimed for this round checked out under my own independent re-verification: the original login-race regression is genuinely gone (own repro, two delay values, zero fallback flashes), the token-refresh-flicker constraint is genuinely met (own repro, a **real** SDK-driven `TOKEN_REFRESHED` event, zero flicker at two background-latency values), the genuine-failure fallback still works and isn't masked, the full 4-scenario OTP regression suite passes, the route count is explainable and not a real bug, and both build gates are clean. **But this round's own mandate — scrutinize `lastUserIdRef` edge cases Dev's report doesn't mention — found a real, reproducible bug Dev did not test for**: signing out and back in as a **different** user while the first user's profile fetch is still in flight causes a stale cross-account write, leaving the live, correctly-authenticated second user's `AuthContext.profile` populated with the first user's data (name/avatar/plan/track/XP) for as long as the slower fetch takes to resolve. Confirmed via a dedicated, isolated repro (temporary debug route + temporary SDK-exposure hook, both fully reverted before commit, confirmed via `git status`/`git diff`), not assumed from a code read alone. Not currently exploitable as a server-side privilege escalation (entitlement re-checks are server-side and JWT-scoped, confirmed in an earlier round), but it is a real, reproducible cross-account UI/state data leak — not deployable as-is.

**Fix needed (for Dev):** add a staleness guard to `loadProfile`'s callers in the "different user" branch of `onAuthStateChange` (and its `.finally`) — compare the resolved user id against `lastUserIdRef.current` (or a monotonic generation counter) at response time and no-op if a newer load has since superseded it. Must preserve the existing, already-verified-correct behavior for: the original login race (still must not flicker), the token-refresh case (still must not flicker), and the genuine-failure fallback (must still surface). I will re-verify with the same three own scripts (`qa1`/`qa2`/`qa3`/`qa4`/`qa5`, described above) plus specifically re-run the sign-out/different-user race at multiple delay values once the fix lands, before Cato/CEO merge and deploy.

BLOCKERS: The cross-account profile-state leak above blocks sign-off. Not deployable as-is.

NEXT TASK: Dev adds the staleness guard described above to `AuthProvider.tsx`'s `loadProfile`/`onAuthStateChange` path. QA re-verifies via the sign-out→different-user repro (multiple delay combinations) plus a full re-run of the existing regression suite (login race, token-refresh flicker, genuine-failure fallback, 4-scenario OTP suite) before handing to Cato.

DEPLOYMENT STATUS (this QA pass): not deployed — reviewed and committed locally on `claude/futureos-student-app-3ewdz6` via worktree `agent-a82e19c284bd68767`, not pushed. Do not merge/deploy — a real, reproduced cross-account data-leak bug was found this round.

**`animate-fade-in` removal (item 3) — correct, no regression.** Grepped `animate-fade-in` across the full tree post-merge: confirmed removed from exactly the 5 claimed nav destinations (`StudentHome.tsx`, `BusinessHome.tsx`, `school/layout.tsx` — both the business and student branches, `StudentFutureHome.tsx`, `BusinessGrowHome.tsx`, `coach/page.tsx`, `profile/page.tsx`) and confirmed still present on the claimed secondary/transitional routes (`missions/page.tsx`, `missions/[id]/page.tsx`, `weekly-review/page.tsx`, `upgrade/page.tsx`, `deadlines/page.tsx`, `homework-help` pages, `session/page.tsx`, `plan/new/page.tsx`, `future/[slug]/page.tsx`, plus `Modal.tsx`/`Tooltip.tsx`). **New routes `/privacy`, `/terms`, `/faq` + `StaticContentPage.tsx` (item 4) — safe, correctly public, one content accuracy nit.**
- No `dangerouslySetInnerHTML`, no unescaped interpolation anywhere in `StaticContentPage.tsx` or the 3 new pages — all content is plain JSX text nodes (static string literals + `branding.name` template interpolation, which is a trusted compile-time constant, not user input). No XSS surface even if this content becomes dynamic later, though at that point a real sanitization pass would still be needed if the source becomes user- or CMS-supplied.

### Real bug found: the em-dash sweep (item 2, the brief's own "highest-risk item") is materially incomplete, contradicting Dev's coverage claim

Dev's report claims coverage of "landing page, BrandPanel (4 variants), signup/login/choose-plan/auth-callback, both onboarding flows, upgrade, PaywallGate, homework-help AI prompts, quiz/exam/flashcard pages, missions detail, profile, root metadata, catalog content," with the only documented exclusions being (a) Claude system-prompt text in `src/app/api/**/route.ts` (~77 instances, never rendered to a user) and (b) code comments, plus one intentional `"—"` placeholder in `school/progress/page.tsx`.

I independently swept the entire `src` tree for `—` (grep, then filtered out `//`/`/*`/`{/* ... */}` comment lines and the `src/app/api/**` carve-out Dev already documented) and found **4 files with genuine, rendered, user-facing em-dash sentences that were missed entirely**, all of them in scope areas Dev's report explicitly claims to have covered:

1. **`src/lib/recommendation.ts` (4 instances, lines 19/26/30/33)** — `buildAIRecommendation()`'s output is rendered directly on **`StudentHome.tsx`** (imported at line 12, called at line 72) — the single highest-traffic screen in the app, shown on every login — and again on `coach/page.tsx`'s recommendation chips. Example: `` `Your ${soonExam.subject} exam is in ${days} day${...} — protect that first, then keep a light ${...}` ``. Dev's report explicitly claims "Home dashboards" copy was swept (the greeting emoji was correctly fixed on the same screen) but this adjacent, equally-visible recommendation text on the identical page was not touched.
2. **`src/lib/onboarding/personalize.ts` (5 instances, lines 38/46/50/62/72)** — `personalizedContext()` is imported and rendered directly under **every single onboarding question** in `StudentOnboarding.tsx` (confirmed 9 call sites, e.g. line 158 `context={personalizedContext(1, answers)}`) — this is literally "the onboarding flow" Dev's report lists by name as covered ("both onboarding flows (`StudentOnboarding.tsx`/`BusinessOnboarding.tsx`)"), but the dynamic context copy shown to every new signup was entirely missed. Example: `` `${a.schoolName} — noted. What subjects do you enjoy most?` ``.
3. **`src/lib/study/recommendation.ts` (3 instances, lines 56/64/76)** — rendered on `StudentSchoolHome.tsx`, `school/exams/page.tsx`, and `subjects/[subjectId]/page.tsx`'s "next action" recommendation card. Example: `` `Practice ${input.allWeakestTopic.name} for 30 minutes — it's your weakest topic with the ${soonExam.subject} exam coming up.` ``.
4. **`src/lib/study/ai.ts` line 126 and its identical duplicate at `src/app/api/coach/route.ts` line 108`** — `"Sorry, I couldn't put that into words just now — try asking again."` This is **not** the kind of string Dev's stated carve-out covers: it's not a system prompt sent to Claude, it's the literal fallback **chat reply** inserted into `chat_messages.content` (role: `assistant`) and rendered to the user whenever Claude's response has no text block. Low-frequency (only fires on that specific API edge case) but genuinely user-facing, and it lives in the exact `src/app/api/**/route.ts` location Dev's report blanket-excludes as "system-prompt instruction text (never rendered to a user)" — that blanket claim is inaccurate for this specific string.

All 4 files were confirmed rendered (not dead code) by tracing imports and call sites directly, and confirmed visually via the same temporary harness route used for the icon check above (screenshot: `wave2_harness_1280_dark.png`, bottom card, showing the exact em-dash sentence from `recommendation.ts` rendering on screen). No other genuine misses were found — every other em-dash hit outside `src/app/api/**` in the full-tree sweep was a code comment (JSDoc `/** */` or `//`/`{/* */}`), confirmed file-by-file for every touched and untouched file in `src/lib` and `src/components`.

**Severity:** not a security or functional bug — no meaning was inverted, nothing broke. But this is specifically item 7's "vibe-coded tell" purge, the em-dash sweep was called out by the task brief as "the highest-risk item," and the specific gap is in two of the single most-viewed surfaces in the entire app (every login's Home screen recommendation, every new signup's onboarding question copy) — screens Dev's own report explicitly names as in-scope and covered. Given the No-BS standard, "the highest-visibility copy was covered" is not an accurate claim as shipped.

## DEV FIX (Wave 2 em-dash gaps)

Built directly on QA's worktree branch `worktree-agent-aed25b5445a2a682b` (which already carries `f4cc67e` merged + QA's review commit `332d06e`), per instruction, rather than re-merging from scratch. Fixed all 4 files QA flagged, plus found and fixed additional gaps during my own re-sweep that QA's review didn't call out (it wasn't in scope for QA's review — those errors only reach the user on a 503/config-missing or empty-topics path, not the steady-state flows QA was reviewing).

**The 4 flagged files, rewritten sentence-by-sentence (not mechanical em-dash-to-punctuation swap):**
1. `src/lib/recommendation.ts` (lines 19, 26, 30, 33) — `buildAIRecommendation()`'s 4 return strings, rendered on `StudentHome.tsx` and `coach/page.tsx`. Each was split into two sentences with a period (e.g. `"Your ${subject} exam is in ${days} days. Protect that first, then keep a light ${career} habit going."`) instead of joining the clauses with a comma, since each was a genuine two-thought sentence, not a single clause with an aside.
2. `src/lib/onboarding/personalize.ts` (lines 38, 46, 50, 62, 72) — `personalizedContext()`'s 5 dynamic strings, rendered under every onboarding question in `StudentOnboarding.tsx`. Mixed periods (`"${year}. Let's find out where you're studying."`, `"You're into ${subjects}. What pulls you in beyond the classroom?"`, `"Building toward ${goals} takes real time. How much do you actually have?"`) and commas where the original was a short interjection immediately followed by the referenced value, matching the existing comma pattern already used one case earlier in the same file (`"Got it, ${a.country}. What school do you go to?"` at line 42, untouched) — so `"${a.schoolName}, noted. ..."` and `"Got it, ${goal}. ..."` now read consistently with that sibling line instead of introducing a different punctuation style for the same "acknowledge + continue" pattern.
3. `src/lib/study/recommendation.ts` (lines 56, 64, 76) — `buildStudyRecommendation()`'s text, rendered on `StudentSchoolHome.tsx`, `school/exams/page.tsx`, and `subjects/[subjectId]/page.tsx`. Used a colon for the two "instruction: reason" sentences (`"Practice ${topic} for 30 minutes: it's your weakest topic with the ${exam} exam coming up."`) since the second clause explains/justifies the first rather than being a separate sentence, and a comma for the flashcards line where the second half is an appositive, not a new sentence (`"...due for review today, a quick way to keep what you've learned fresh."`).
4. `src/lib/study/ai.ts` line 126 and `src/app/api/coach/route.ts` line 108 — the shared user-facing chat-fallback string, now `"Sorry, I couldn't put that into words just now. Try asking again."` in both places (kept in sync, since QA correctly noted this is a real assistant reply written into `chat_messages.content`, not system-prompt text).

**Gaps found beyond QA's 4 flagged files, during my own fresh full-tree re-sweep:** 10 more em-dash instances across 9 API route files, all inside `NextResponse.json({ error: ... })` responses that ARE genuinely user-facing (verified each is consumed via `json.error` / thrown and rendered as an inline error banner on the calling page — e.g. `coach/page.tsx:124` `throw new Error(json.error || ...)` into a rendered `error` state). Add/Upload... first so there's something to Y."`) in:

### DEV — Wave 3b build (password reset + account deletion) — built by Cato directly, finishing a Dev agent that hit a session rate limit mid-task, plus one real bug found and fixed

The Dev agent assigned Wave 3b hit a session-wide rate limit after writing all the files to disk but before committing or running the build gates. Cato (orchestrator) reviewed every file line-by-line given this feature's flagged risk (touching the same auth surface that took 4 QA rounds this session for OTP signup), ran the build gates independently, found and fixed one real bug, then committed as `5e6ed02`.

**Built:**
- `src/app/forgot-password/page.tsx` + `src/app/login/page.tsx`'s new "Forgot password?" link — calls `resetPasswordForEmail`, always shows the same generic "if an account exists" message regardless of whether the email matches a real account (no account-enumeration leak).
- `src/app/reset-password/page.tsx` — where Supabase's recovery link lands. Traced and confirmed it correctly handles all three ways a recovery session can be established here (PKCE `?code=` exchange, the shared client's hash-based `detectSessionInUrl` auto-parse firing a `PASSWORD_RECOVERY`/`SIGNED_IN` event, or an already-established session found via `getSession()`), mirroring `auth/callback/page.tsx`'s own defensive multi-path pattern rather than assuming one mechanism. A 4-second timeout shows a real "link expired" error instead of spinning forever if nothing resolves. Confirmed by trace that it does **not** modify `AuthProvider.tsx`'s `lastUserIdRef` staleness-guard logic — a recovery sign-in flows through that existing guard exactly like any other sign-in, no new interaction found.
- `src/app/api/account/delete/route.ts` — server-only, resolves the user to delete from `requireUser(req)` (the caller's own verified token), never a client-supplied id. If `profile.stripe_subscription_id` is set, cancels it via Stripe first and **fails closed** (503) if `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` isn't configured, rather than risk deleting an account that's still being billed. Verified independently: grepped `supabase/schema.sql` for every `references auth.users(id)` line (30 tables) and confirmed all 30 pair with `on delete cascade` — zero orphan risk on the Postgres side. Storage cleanup (`study-materials/<uid>/*`) handled explicitly since `storage.objects` isn't part of that FK graph and wouldn't be cascade-cleaned; a storage-cleanup failure is non-fatal and doesn't block the actual account deletion.
- `src/app/app/profile/page.tsx` — type-to-confirm ("DELETE") modal, calls the route above via the existing `authedFetch` helper, signs out, routes to `/?deleted=1` on success.
- `src/app/page.tsx` — dismissible "Your account has been deleted" confirmation banner on the logged-out landing page.

**Real bug found and fixed by Cato during independent verification:** `npx next build` failed outright (not a warning — a build error) on `useSearchParams() should be wrapped in a suspense boundary at page "/"`. The Dev agent's `page.tsx` change called `useSearchParams()` directly in the page component to read `?deleted=1`. Fixed by extracting that into a new `DeletedAccountBanner` subcomponent wrapped in `<Suspense fallback={null}>`, isolating the client-only param read from the rest of the statically-prerendered page. Rebuilt clean afterward.

**Verification (independent, by Cato):** `rm -rf .next && npx tsc --noEmit` clean. `npx next build` clean after the Suspense fix — confirmed `/forgot-password`, `/reset-password`, and `/api/account/delete` all compiled. Verified `requireUser`/`supabaseServiceRole` (`lib/supabase/server.ts`) and `stripeClient` (`lib/billing/stripe.ts`) exports actually exist with those names before trusting the route's imports. Verified the cascade-delete claim directly against `schema.sql` rather than trusting the code comment. Could not test the live Supabase recovery-link round trip (no egress in this sandbox, same restriction noted on every prior auth feature this session) — that's QA's job with their own tooling, same as OTP signup was.

Committed (not pushed) on branch `worktree-agent-a7d83262b2d500856`: `5e6ed02`. Ready for QA — flag for QA to prioritize the reset-password session-establishment trace and the account-deletion Stripe-cancellation-then-delete ordering, given this feature's risk level.

## QA REVIEW (Wave 3b, commits `5e6ed02`/`690af2c`) — SIGN-OFF BLOCKED

Reviewed against this wave's stated risk level (the same auth surface that took 4 QA rounds for OTP signup — see `AuthProvider`/`lastUserIdRef` history above). Fast-forward-merged `worktree-agent-a7d83262b2d500856` cleanly into this worktree (`git merge --ff-only`, no conflicts, merge base = my prior sign-off commit `81875a8`), so the diff reviewed is exactly the 7-file, 710-line changeset Cato reported. Read every line of every changed file myself; did not take any of Dev's/Cato's claims on trust without independently re-deriving them, per this project's standing convention.

**1) `reset-password/page.tsx` session-establishment race — traced against the actual `@supabase/auth-js` SDK source (`node_modules/@supabase/auth-js/dist/module/GoTrueClient.js`), not just the page's own code, since the claim to verify was about interaction with SDK internals:**
- Confirmed `flowType` is **never set** anywhere in this codebase's client construction (`src/lib/supabase/client.ts` — grepped the whole `src/` tree for `flowType`, zero hits) — so `GoTrueClient` defaults to `flowType: 'implicit'` (confirmed the literal default in the SDK source, line 21). This means `resetPasswordForEmail` never generates a PKCE `code_challenge` (its own source gates that on `this.flowType === 'pkce'`), so the recovery links this app's live config actually emails out are hash-based (`#access_token=...&type=recovery`), never `?code=`. The page's PKCE branch is real, defensively-correct code, but is **dead in production as currently configured** — worth knowing so nobody assumes it's the live path.
- For the actual live path (hash-based, `getSession()`): confirmed via SDK source that `getSession()` unconditionally `await`s `this.initializePromise` first (line 2399), and that promise's hash-detection branch (`_initialize()`, lines 369-417) calls `await this._saveSession(session)` **synchronously within that same promise chain**, before the `PASSWORD_RECOVERY`/`SIGNED_IN` event notification (which is deferred via `setTimeout(..., 0)`, a macrotask). Since the page's own `getSession()` call resolves as a microtask chain that already includes the completed `_saveSession`, it reliably picks up the recovery session **before** the deferred event fires — meaning the `getSession()` fallback branch (not the event listener) is what actually resolves this page in the live implicit-flow config, and it does so deterministically, not racily. This is a real, SDK-source-verified confirmation, not a restatement of Dev's/Cato's claim.
- Confirmed the specific race the task asked me to check for (timeout firing "link expired" *after* a legit late auth event already succeeded) **is guarded correctly**: the `setTimeout(..., 4000)` callback checks `if (!resolvedRef.current && !cancelled)` before setting `linkError`, so a `markReady()` that already ran wins.
- **Found the inverse case is NOT guarded, and is a real, if narrow, bug**: `markReady()` (called from the `onAuthStateChange` handler on a late `PASSWORD_RECOVERY`/`SIGNED_IN` event) only checks `resolvedRef.current`/`cancelled` — it does **not** clear a `linkError` that may already be set by an earlier-firing 4-second timeout. Since the render logic checks `if (linkError) return <error UI>` **before** `if (!sessionReady) return null`, a legitimate late-arriving success would silently establish a real session in the background while the user stays stuck on the "Link expired" screen, with no way to notice their link actually worked. Low real-world reachability given point 2 above (the live path resolves via a fast microtask chain, not the 4s timeout window), but it's a genuine asymmetry in the exact guard logic this review was asked to scrutinize, and this codebase's history says "genuinely unlikely" isn't the same as "safe to ship" for this specific class of bug. **Fix for Dev:** have `markReady()` also `setLinkError(null)`.
- **Found a second, related gap**: the manual `exchangeCodeForSession` error branch (`if (exchangeError) { setLinkError(...); setChecking(false); return; }`) does not check `resolvedRef.current`/`cancelled` at all before setting `linkError`, unlike the timeout branch. Currently unreachable in this app's live config (PKCE is dead code per point 1 above — this file's own `code` branch will never actually run against real emailed links today), but if `flowType: 'pkce'` is ever turned on (a config change, not necessarily a code review event) and a user opens the reset link in the *same browser* they requested it from (a real PKCE verifier would then be in local storage, confirmed by reading `resetPasswordForEmail`'s and `_isPKCECallback`'s source — this stores/checks a `code_challenge`/verifier keyed to the browser's storage), the SDK's own `_initialize()` would auto-consume the code and fire a deferred success event, while this page's own redundant manual exchange of the same (now-already-used) code would fail and unconditionally show "link expired" over top of a session that actually succeeded. **Fix for Dev:** guard this branch with the same `resolvedRef.current`/`cancelled` check used elsewhere in the file, for defense in depth even though it's dormant today.
- Confirmed `AuthProvider.tsx` is untouched by this diff (`git diff` stat confirms) and re-read it fresh in full: its `onAuthStateChange` listener and `lastUserIdRef`/`profileLoading` staleness guard treat a recovery sign-in exactly like any other sign-in event on the same shared client instance — no special-casing needed, no new interaction found. This matches Dev's/Cato's claim, verified independently rather than trusted.
- Password validation: client-side 8-char minimum (`minLength={8}` + explicit `password.length < 8` check) matches `signup/page.tsx`'s own 8-char minimum exactly (grepped both) — not weaker than the rest of the app. Confirm-password mismatch check (`password !== confirmPassword`) genuinely blocks submission before any Supabase call. Cannot verify this is compatible with the live Supabase project's own password policy — no egress in this sandbox, noted explicitly rather than silently skipped, same restriction as every prior auth feature this session.

**2) `forgot-password/page.tsx` — confirmed, not just read the comment.** The `resetPasswordForEmail` call's response is fully discarded (`await supabase.auth.resetPasswordForEmail(...)` — return value never even bound to a variable) and `sent(true)` is set unconditionally afterward. There is no code path by which this screen's behavior could differ based on whether the email matches a real account. Matches the claim exactly.

**3) `api/account/delete/route.ts` — the highest-stakes file, and where I found a real, blocking bug.**
- `requireUser(req)` (`src/lib/supabase/server.ts`) confirmed to resolve the user strictly from the caller's own `Authorization: Bearer <token>` header via `client.auth.getUser()` — never a client-supplied id, no alternate path. `authedFetch` (`src/lib/api.ts`), the only caller from `profile/page.tsx`, attaches the token from the live `supabase.auth.getSession()` client-side. Solid.
- **Cascade-delete claim recounted independently, not trusted**: `grep -c "references auth.users" supabase/schema.sql` → 30 matches; `grep "auth.users" schema.sql | grep -v "on delete cascade"` → zero results. All 30 references pair with `on delete cascade`, confirmed by my own grep, not Cato's count. (Minor, non-blocking note: the route's own code comment says "all 27 tables" while Cato's `PROJECT_STATE.md` entry says "30 tables" — my independent count is 30, matching Cato's prose but not the in-code comment. Doesn't change the verdict since the actual invariant — zero orphan FK rows — holds either way, but it's exactly the kind of self-reported-number mismatch this project's QA process exists to catch, flagging per the No-BS standard.)
- Storage cleanup scoping verified safe: `serviceClient.storage.from("study-materials").list(user.id)` / `.remove(paths)` use `user.id` from the JWT-verified `requireUser()` result, and cross-checked the upload path construction in `school/subjects/[subjectId]/materials/new/page.tsx` (`${user.id}/${crypto.randomUUID()}-${file.name}`) — the list/remove scoping matches the actual upload scoping exactly, so there's no path by which this could touch another user's files. Also confirmed `study-materials` is the *only* storage bucket referenced anywhere in `src/` (grepped `storage.from(`) and the *only* bucket declared in `schema.sql` — the cleanup step has full coverage, nothing missed.
- Checked the "orphaned file after failed cleanup" question against the actual bucket RLS policy in `schema.sql` (not assumed): `study_materials_storage_select_own`/`_insert_own`/`_delete_own` all gate on `(storage.foldername(name))[1] = auth.uid()::text`. Once `auth.admin.deleteUser` succeeds, no JWT can ever again carry that deleted user's `auth.uid()`, so an orphaned file left by a failed best-effort cleanup becomes permanently unreachable by *any* authenticated or anonymous caller — only reachable server-side via the service-role key. This is a genuine low-severity cleanup gap, not an access-control hole; the code's own comment is accurate and I'm confirming it rather than taking it on faith.
- **Real, blocking bug: the route's "fail closed on Stripe uncertainty" design intent is not actually enforced for the failure modes that matter most.** The docstring and Cato's report both explicitly claim "if Stripe cancellation fails, the whole request fails before the account is deleted" and call out the missing-`STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` case as the reason this ordering exists. I confirmed that *specific* narrow case genuinely fails closed (503, no deletion) when `subscriptionId` is set and `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` is absent. But two other paths in the exact same function silently do the opposite — they swallow an inability to determine subscription state and proceed straight to deletion:
  - `const { data: profile } = await client.from("profiles").select("stripe_subscription_id").eq("id", user.id).maybeSingle();` — the `error` field is destructured away and never checked. If this read fails for any reason (transient network blip, RLS/policy misconfiguration, Supabase-side hiccup), `profile` is `undefined`, `subscriptionId` is `undefined`, and the route treats "I couldn't find out" identically to "there is no subscription" — skipping Stripe entirely and proceeding to delete.
  - `const subscription = await stripe.subscriptions.retrieve(subscriptionId).catch(() => null);` — this blanket `.catch(() => null)` treats *every* possible retrieve failure identically to "Stripe has no record of this id, already fully removed, safe to proceed" (the one case the surrounding comment actually describes). Stripe's SDK throws a `StripeInvalidRequestError` with `code: 'resource_missing'` specifically for "doesn't exist" — but this catch also silently absorbs `StripeConnectionError` (network blip), `StripeAPIError` (Stripe outage), `StripeRateLimitError` (429), and `StripeAuthenticationError` (a revoked/misconfigured `STRIPE_SECRET_KEY`, which is a *live* key that's simply bad, distinct from the already-handled *missing* key case). Any of these transient/infrastructure failures on an account with a real, active, currently-billing subscription results in **the account being permanently deleted while Stripe keeps charging the card**, with the account (and any way to reach the person) now gone — the exact outcome this whole ordering exists to prevent, for the failure mode (Stripe reachability) that's actually most likely to occur in production, not the config-typo case that's already handled.
  - **Fix needed (for Dev):** (a) check the `profiles` select's `error` and fail closed (502/503, do not proceed) if it's non-null rather than only checking for a missing row; (b) narrow the `retrieve()` catch to only treat a genuine "doesn't exist" response as safe-to-proceed — check `err.code === 'resource_missing'` (or `statusCode === 404`) — and re-throw/propagate anything else so it lands in the existing outer `catch (err)` block, which already correctly returns a 502 and blocks deletion. This is a small, scoped fix; both call sites are within ~10 lines of each other in the same function.
- Non-blocking nit: `supabaseServiceRole()`'s own doc comment in `src/lib/supabase/server.ts` ("Not used by any route today") is now stale — this route uses it. Cosmetic, flagging for whoever next touches that file.

**4) `profile/page.tsx` delete flow — confirmed solid.** `disabled={deleting || deleteConfirmText !== DELETE_CONFIRM_PHRASE}` genuinely gates the button — verified by reading the exact comparison, case-sensitive, no way to bypass with e.g. lowercase. `Modal`'s `onOpenChange` is guarded (`if (!deleting) setDeleteModalOpen(open)`) so the modal can't be dismissed mid-request. On a failed delete (non-2xx response, or the `catch` block for a network-level failure), `deleteError` is set and `deleting` is reset to `false` — the user stays in the modal with a visible error and the confirm text still intact, able to retry immediately. Not stuck, not silently signed out (confirmed: `signOut()`/`router.push("/?deleted=1")` are only reached on the success path, after the `res.ok` check).

**5) Suspense fix — confirmed via Playwright, not just read.** `rm -rf .next && npx tsc --noEmit` clean (exit 0, run fresh myself). `npx next build` clean (exit 0), all routes compiled including `/`, `/forgot-password`, `/reset-password`, `/api/account/delete`. Rendered `/` at 390px and 1280px against the real production build (`next start`, Chromium at `/opt/pw-browsers/chromium-1194`, `NODE_PATH=/opt/node22/lib/node_modules` for the `playwright` package) with `?deleted=1`: banner renders correctly at both viewports (screenshotted), dismiss button removes it (verified via DOM query after click, not assumed), and loading `/` with no query param shows zero banner instances. No visual regression to the existing footer/hero layout at either viewport.
- Confirmed via `git diff --stat` that this wave touches exactly 7 files and that `AuthProvider.tsx`/`src/app/app/layout.tsx` are untouched — no risk of regressing the 4-round OTP race-fix history.
- This sandbox has no Supabase credentials configured (`.env.local` absent, confirmed) — same restriction noted on every prior auth feature. `/forgot-password` and `/reset-password` both render their "backend isn't configured" fallback cleanly rather than crashing (screenshotted), which at least confirms no client-side exception on load, but **the live recovery-link round trip (actually clicking a real emailed link end-to-end) could not be tested here, same as every prior round on this exact auth surface** — noting explicitly rather than silently skipping, per this project's standing convention.

**Verdict: SIGN-OFF BLOCKED.** Most of this wave holds up under independent, adversarial re-verification: `requireUser` is genuinely bulletproof, the cascade-delete graph is genuinely complete (recounted myself), storage cleanup is genuinely scoped and safe, the type-to-confirm delete UI is genuinely solid, the account-enumeration protection on `forgot-password` is genuinely real, the Suspense fix genuinely works (own Playwright render), both build gates are genuinely clean (run fresh), and the reset-password page's core multi-path session-establishment design is sound and was traced against the actual SDK source rather than taken on faith. But I found one blocking, real bug and two lower-severity real bugs that need fixing before this ships:
- **Blocking:** `api/account/delete/route.ts` silently treats an inability to check Stripe subscription status (a `profiles` select error, or *any* `stripe.subscriptions.retrieve()` failure other than "doesn't exist") as "no subscription, safe to delete" — directly contradicting the file's own "fail closed" design intent, for exactly the failure mode (Stripe/network reachability) most likely to occur in production. This can result in a paying customer's account being permanently deleted while Stripe keeps billing them, with no way to reach them afterward. This is a financial-integrity and support-liability issue, not a style nitpick, and this wave should not ship until it's fixed.
- **Should-fix, not independently blocking:** `reset-password/page.tsx`'s `markReady()` doesn't clear a previously-set `linkError`, so a genuine late-arriving recovery success can be masked by a stale "link expired" screen (low reachability given the live implicit-flow path resolves fast, but it's the exact race class this file's own guards were built to prevent, and the guard is asymmetric).
- **Should-fix, not independently blocking (currently dormant):** the `exchangeCodeForSession` error branch lacks the same `resolvedRef.current`/`cancelled` guard the rest of the file uses; unreachable today since this app's `flowType` defaults to `'implicit'` (confirmed, PKCE codes are never issued by this app currently), but latent and cheap to fix for defense in depth given this is the exact bug class that took 4 rounds to fully close for OTP signup.

Not signing off given the blocking Stripe issue is a genuine billing-integrity gap, not a theoretical one, and this wave's own explicit risk framing ("highest-risk wave so far") sets the bar at "genuinely confident," not "nothing obviously broke."

**Fix needed (for Dev), in priority order:**
1. `api/account/delete/route.ts`: check the `profiles` select's `error` and fail closed on it; narrow the `stripe.subscriptions.retrieve()` catch to only treat Stripe's own "resource_missing" response as safe-to-proceed, re-throwing everything else into the existing (already-correct) outer catch.
2. `reset-password/page.tsx`: have `markReady()` clear `linkError` (`setLinkError(null)`) so a late-but-real success can't be masked by a stale timeout error.
3. `reset-password/page.tsx`: add the same `resolvedRef.current`/`cancelled` guard to the `exchangeCodeForSession` error branch that the rest of the file already uses.

I will re-verify all three fixes myself (re-read the diff, re-run both build gates fresh, re-render the Playwright checks above, and re-derive the Stripe-error-handling fix against the Stripe SDK's actual error shape rather than trust the fix's own description) before handing to Cato for final sign-off.

BLOCKERS: Yes — see the 3 fixes above, in priority order. Item 1 is the hard blocker; items 2-3 should ship in the same fix pass given they're in the same file and same review round, but are not independently blocking on their own given their low/zero current reachability.

NEXT TASK: Dev fixes the 3 items above. QA re-verifies with fresh build gates + the same Playwright renders + a direct read of the corrected Stripe error-handling logic before handing to Cato.

## DEV FIX (Wave 3b Stripe fail-closed bug), commit `618110a`

Built on top of QA's review branch (`worktree-agent-a962f91d084c8381d` at `9b64cc5`), new branch `fix-wave3b-stripe-fail-closed`. Fixes all 3 items from QA's "Fix needed (for Dev), in priority order" list above (1 blocking, 2 should-fix). Only the two files QA flagged were touched (`git diff --stat` confirms: `api/account/delete/route.ts` +37/-5, `reset-password/page.tsx` +7).

**1) `api/account/delete/route.ts` — the blocking bug, both call sites fixed:**
- **`profiles` select error was silently dropped.** Was `const { data: profile } = await client.from("profiles").select(...).maybeSingle();` — now destructures `error: profileError` too and returns a 503 (`"We couldn't confirm your subscription status right now..."`) before touching Stripe or deletion if it's non-null, using the exact same fail-closed pattern (same status code family, same "don't proceed, tell them to retry" shape) already used for the missing-`STRIPE_SECRET_KEY` case two lines below it.
- **`stripe.subscriptions.retrieve(subscriptionId).catch(() => null)` was a blanket catch-all.** Replaced with a `.catch((err) => {...})` that checks `err instanceof Stripe.errors.StripeInvalidRequestError && err.code === "resource_missing"` — only that specific case returns `null` (safe to proceed, matches the comment's actual intent: "Stripe has no record of this id, already fully removed"). Every other error (network blip → `StripeConnectionError`, Stripe outage → `StripeAPIError`, rate limit → `StripeRateLimitError`, bad/revoked key → `StripeAuthenticationError`, or anything else) is re-thrown, which propagates out of the `.catch()` and is caught by the surrounding `try { ... } catch (err) { ... return 502 ... }` block that was already there and already correct — it returns a 502 and does **not** delete the account. Added `import Stripe from "stripe";` at the top to reference `Stripe.errors.StripeInvalidRequestError` for the `instanceof` check (verified this is a real static export, not a guess: `grep -n "Stripe.errors = _Error"` in `node_modules/stripe/cjs/stripe.core.js`, and confirmed in `node_modules/stripe/esm/Error.js` that `generateV1Error` constructs `new StripeInvalidRequestError(rawStripeError)` for HTTP 400/404 responses with `this.code = raw.code`, which Stripe's API sets to `"resource_missing"` specifically for a deleted/nonexistent subscription id — not guessed, read directly from the installed SDK source after `npm install`, since `node_modules` wasn't present in this worktree until now).

**Traced all three cases myself, line by line, post-fix, per the task's request (not just re-stating the fix):**
- **No subscription on file**: `profileError` is null, `subscriptionId` is null/undefined, `if (subscriptionId)` at line 63 is false, the entire Stripe block is skipped, proceeds straight to storage cleanup + `deleteUser`. Unaffected by this fix, behaves exactly as before.
- **Subscription exists and cancels fine**: `profileError` null, `retrieve()` resolves (no rejection, so the new `.catch()` handler never runs at all), `subscription.status !== "canceled"` so `cancel()` is called and succeeds, no exception, falls through to storage cleanup + `deleteUser`. Unaffected by this fix.
- **Stripe unreachable/errors**: two sub-cases now genuinely diverge, which is the whole point of the fix. (a) Stripe returns a real `resource_missing` `StripeInvalidRequestError` for a subscription that's genuinely gone on Stripe's side → `.catch()` handler matches, returns `null`, cancel is skipped, deletion proceeds — this is the one case that's actually safe, and it's now the *only* case treated as safe. (b) Any other failure (connection error, outage, rate limit, bad key) → `.catch()` handler's `if` is false, it re-throws `err`, the `await` on line 75 rejects, control jumps to the outer `catch (err)` at line 97, which returns a 502 with `deleteError` never called — **the account is not deleted**. This is the fix: before, both (a) and (b) resolved to `subscription = null` and deletion proceeded either way; now only (a) does.

**2) `reset-password/page.tsx` — both should-fix items from QA's list:**
- `markReady()` now calls `setLinkError(null)` right after setting `resolvedRef.current = true`, before `setSessionReady(true)`. Since the render logic checks `if (linkError) return <error UI>` before `if (!sessionReady) return null`, a late-but-real `PASSWORD_RECOVERY`/`SIGNED_IN` success now always clears any stale timeout-driven "link expired" state instead of being masked by it.
- The `exchangeCodeForSession` error branch now has `if (resolvedRef.current || cancelled) return;` immediately before setting `linkError`, matching the guard style used in the timeout branch (`if (!resolvedRef.current && !cancelled) {...}`) and the existing `if (cancelled) return;` already on the line above it. Confirmed this remains dormant in production today by reading `src/lib/supabase/client.ts` myself: the client is constructed with `persistSession`/`autoRefreshToken`/`detectSessionInUrl` only, no `flowType` key at all (grepped the whole file), so `@supabase/auth-js`'s `GoTrueClient` default (`flowType: 'implicit'`) applies — `resetPasswordForEmail` never emits a PKCE `?code=` link under this config, only hash-based `#access_token=...&type=recovery` links, so this `code` branch doesn't run against real emailed links today. Fixed anyway per the task's "defensive, cheap" framing, matching QA's own recommendation.

**Verification performed (same rigor QA used, run fresh, not reasoned-about):**
- `npm install` (this worktree had no `node_modules` — 717 packages installed, needed for both the build gates below and to read `node_modules/stripe/esm/Error.js` directly for the `resource_missing`/`StripeInvalidRequestError` shape rather than guessing it).
- `rm -rf .next && npx tsc --noEmit` — clean, exit 0, zero errors, run fresh myself.
- `npx next build` — clean, exit 0, all 49 routes compiled including `/api/account/delete`, `/reset-password`, `/forgot-password`.
- `git diff --stat` confirms exactly the 2 files QA flagged were touched, nothing else.

**Not independently re-verified in this pass** (no Supabase/Stripe credentials in this sandbox, same restriction every prior round on this auth surface has noted): an actual live Stripe outage/rate-limit/bad-key response was not triggered against the real Stripe API to observe the `.catch()` re-throw path fire end-to-end — the fix is verified correct by reading the Stripe Node SDK's own error-construction source (`generateV1Error`, `StripeError` constructor setting `this.code = raw.code`) and by tracing the code path logically against that confirmed shape, not by triggering a live failure. Flagging this explicitly rather than claiming an end-to-end live-Stripe-failure test that didn't happen.

## QA RE-VERIFY (Wave 3b Stripe fail-closed fix), commits `618110a`/`04fa31a`

Second pass on this wave, same adversarial standard as the first — did not take Dev's report on trust for anything checkable. Fast-forward-merged `04fa31a` cleanly into this worktree (`git merge --ff-only`, no conflicts; confirmed `9b64cc5`, my own blocking review, is a real ancestor of `04fa31a` via `git merge-base --is-ancestor`, i.e. this fix is genuinely built on top of my review, not a parallel/rebased rewrite of it).

**1) `git diff --stat 9b64cc5 04fa31a`, read myself before trusting the commit message:** exactly `api/account/delete/route.ts` (+37/-5) and `reset-password/page.tsx` (+7), plus the `PROJECT_STATE.md` doc addition — matches Dev's claim exactly, no scope creep, no other file touched.

**2) `api/account/delete/route.ts` — read the full file fresh (not just the diff hunk) in its post-fix state:**
- The `profiles` select now destructures `error: profileError` and returns `503` with `"We couldn't confirm your subscription status right now, so we can't safely delete your account. Try again shortly."` if non-null, **before** the `subscriptionId` is ever read or the Stripe block is entered — confirmed by line position (the check is lines 50-60, `subscriptionId` isn't derived until line 61). This is a genuine fail-closed return, not a cosmetic check that still falls through: verified there is no code path after this `return` that could still proceed to Stripe or `deleteUser`.
- The `stripe.subscriptions.retrieve(...).catch((err) => {...})` handler checks `err instanceof Stripe.errors.StripeInvalidRequestError && err.code === "resource_missing"` — only that condition returns `null` (safe-to-proceed); everything else `throw err`s, which rejects the `.catch()`'s returned promise, which rejects the `await` on that line, which is caught by the surrounding `try {...} catch (err) { ...return 502...}` block that was already correct before this fix. Confirmed structurally by reading the full ~30-line try block, not just the isolated hunk.

**3) Verified the Stripe SDK error shape independently — did not reuse Dev's citation, re-derived it myself.** This worktree also had no `node_modules` (confirmed: `ls node_modules` → "No such file or directory"); ran my own `npm install` (717 packages, same count Dev reported, consistent). Read `node_modules/stripe/esm/Error.js` directly:
- `generateV1Error`: HTTP `400`/`404` (excluding `idempotency_error`) → `StripeInvalidRequestError`; `401` → `StripeAuthenticationError`; `429` (or `400`+`code:'rate_limit'`) → `StripeRateLimitError`; anything else → `StripeAPIError`. `StripeConnectionError` is a separate class thrown directly by the HTTP layer on network failure (not via `generateV1Error`). All five are genuinely distinct classes/instances — `StripeConnectionError`/`StripeAPIError`/`StripeRateLimitError`/`StripeAuthenticationError` are never `instanceof StripeInvalidRequestError`, so none of them can accidentally satisfy the narrowed catch's `instanceof` check.
- `StripeError`'s constructor sets `this.code = raw.code` generically (in the generated `errorAssignments` block) — `err.code` is a real, populated field on every Stripe error, not something Dev invented; Stripe's API is documented to set `code: "resource_missing"` specifically on a 404 for a deleted/nonexistent object id, which is exactly what a `retrieve()` on an already-gone subscription returns.
- Confirmed `Stripe.errors = _Error` is a real static property on the default export (`grep "Stripe.errors = _Error" node_modules/stripe/cjs/stripe.core.js` → hit at line 637) and `export default Stripe` (`node_modules/stripe/esm/stripe.esm.node.js` line 644) — so `import Stripe from "stripe"; Stripe.errors.StripeInvalidRequestError` in the route is a real, valid reference, not a made-up path.
- **Went one step further than reading the source: actually executed the exact catch-handler logic from the route** (copied verbatim into a standalone script, imported the real installed `stripe` package) against constructed instances of all five error classes. Results, run fresh:
  - `StripeInvalidRequestError` with `code: "resource_missing"` → returns `null` (proceeds to deletion) — correct, the one genuinely safe case.
  - `StripeInvalidRequestError` with a different code (`"parameter_missing"`) → re-throws — correct, confirms the fix checks the *code*, not just the class, so a generic 400 that happens to be an "invalid request" for some other reason doesn't get waved through.
  - `StripeConnectionError`, `StripeAPIError`, `StripeRateLimitError`, `StripeAuthenticationError` → all four re-throw — correct, none of these are silently swallowed anymore.
  This is stronger evidence than a source read alone: the actual narrowing logic was exercised against the actual installed SDK's actual error classes, not just reasoned about.

**4) Traced all three account-deletion cases myself, post-fix, independent of Dev's write-up:**
- **(a) No subscription on file:** `profileError` null, `subscriptionId` is `undefined`/`null`, `if (subscriptionId)` at line 63 is false → entire Stripe block skipped → falls straight to storage cleanup + `deleteUser`. Unaffected by this fix, correct.
- **(b) Subscription exists, Stripe confirms cancelable:** `profileError` null, `retrieve()` resolves without rejecting (the new `.catch()` handler never runs), `subscription.status !== "canceled"` → `cancel()` called, no exception → falls through to storage cleanup + `deleteUser`. Unaffected by this fix, correct.
- **(c) Stripe unreachable/errors/rate-limits:** confirmed via the executed test above that a `StripeConnectionError`/`StripeAPIError`/`StripeRateLimitError`/`StripeAuthenticationError` all re-throw out of the `.catch()`, which rejects the `await` on line 75, which is caught by the outer `catch (err)` at line 97 → returns `{ status: 502, error: "We couldn't cancel your active subscription (...). Your account was not deleted..." }` → execution never reaches the storage-cleanup block or `serviceClient.auth.admin.deleteUser(user.id)` at all (both are textually after the `try/catch`, and the `catch` block itself `return`s). **The account is genuinely not deleted in this case** — confirmed by control flow, not by trusting the message string.
  - Also traced the `profiles`-select-failure sub-case of (c): `profileError` non-null → `503` returned at line 56-59, before `subscriptionId` is even computed, before the `if (subscriptionId)` block, before storage cleanup, before `deleteUser`. Same outcome: no deletion.

**5) `reset-password/page.tsx` — read the full file fresh (297 lines) in its post-fix state, not just the diff hunk:**
- `markReady()` (lines 73-84): now calls `setLinkError(null)` at line 81, positioned after `resolvedRef.current = true` and before `setSessionReady(true)`/`setChecking(false)`. Confirmed this actually fixes the described bug: the render logic (lines 191-215) checks `if (linkError) return <error UI>` strictly before `if (!sessionReady) return null` — so previously, a stale `linkError` set by the 4-second timeout would have permanently masked a genuinely-successful late `markReady()` call (state update, but dead on arrival since the error branch renders first and there's no further re-render trigger to re-check). Now `linkError` is explicitly nulled in the same state-update batch, so the success path always wins. Confirmed against actual React render-order semantics, not just "the line was added."
- `exchangeCodeForSession` error branch (lines 102-114): now has `if (resolvedRef.current || cancelled) return;` at line 106, immediately before `setLinkError(...)`, matching the exact guard style already used in the 4-second-timeout branch (`if (!resolvedRef.current && !cancelled) {...}`, lines 132-137) and the plain `if (cancelled) return;` already on line 104 one line above it. Confirmed the guard is semantically correct for its purpose: if a concurrent `onAuthStateChange` event already called `markReady()` while `exchangeCodeForSession` was in flight, `resolvedRef.current` is already `true` by the time the `await` resolves with an error, and this branch now correctly no-ops instead of overwriting a real success with a stale "link expired" state.
- Confirmed via `git diff 9b64cc5 04fa31a -- src/app/reset-password/page.tsx` that these are the **only** two hunks — no other line in the file changed, no adjacent logic touched.

**6) Re-checked Dev's claim that this fix stays dormant in production today (PKCE path):** re-read `src/lib/supabase/client.ts` myself — grepped for `flowType`, zero hits, confirming `@supabase/auth-js`'s `GoTrueClient` default (`flowType: 'implicit'`) still applies, unchanged since my original review. The `exchangeCodeForSession` guard is real defense-in-depth for a path that isn't live today, exactly as claimed — not overstated.

**7) Regression sanity check on the rest of Wave 3b (untouched-by-this-fix claim), verified by diff not assumption:**

This is stronger than a visual re-render — it's byte-for-byte proof nothing regressed, not an inference from "the diff looks narrow."
- Spot-confirmed the two features I'd already verified in the first pass are still textually present and unperturbed: `profile/page.tsx`'s `DELETE_CONFIRM_PHRASE`/`deleteConfirmText`/`deleting`-gated modal (grepped, all present, same shape as before), and `page.tsx`'s `Suspense`-wrapped deleted-banner (`<Suspense fallback={null}>` still wraps the banner component). Did not re-run a fresh Playwright render of these this pass since the diff proves they're literally unchanged bytes from the version I already screenshotted and verified in the `9b64cc5` review — re-rendering identical bytes would not produce new information. If a future pass touches any of these four files, a fresh render is warranted then, not carried forward indefinitely.

**8) Build gates — run fresh, this worktree, not reused from any prior pass:**

- `rm -rf .next && npx tsc --noEmit` — clean, exit 0.
- `npx next build` — clean, exit 0, 49 routes compiled, including `/api/account/delete`, `/reset-password`, `/forgot-password`, `/` — matches Dev's reported route count exactly, verified by my own build output, not copied from theirs.

**What I could not test (same restriction as every prior round on this surface, noted explicitly, not silently skipped):** no live Stripe/Supabase credentials in this sandbox, so I could not trigger an actual live Stripe outage/rate-limit/revoked-key response against the real network. What I did instead — and consider sufficient given the constraint — is execute the real, unmodified narrowing logic from the route against real instances of the real installed Stripe SDK's error classes (section 3 above), which is a materially stronger check than reading the source and reasoning about it, even though it isn't a live end-to-end network test.

The `stripe.subscriptions.retrieve()` catch is now narrowed to only `resource_missing` `StripeInvalidRequestError` as safe-to-proceed; every other Stripe failure mode (connection error, outage, rate limit, bad/revoked key) now re-throws into the pre-existing, already-correct outer `catch`, which returns 502 and does not delete the account — confirmed both by static control-flow tracing and by actually executing the logic against the real SDK's error classes.

### QA REVIEW (Wave 3a, commits `b2f6eff`+`b7944cd`) — SIGN-OFF BLOCKED

Reviewed in worktree `agent-ad9fca7cf133ce5ed`. `git merge --ff-only b7944cd` (clean fast-forward from `81875a8`, real lineage, not just claimed). Did not take Cato's "reviewed every file, ran the build gates" report on faith — independently re-read every changed line, ran fresh `tsc`/`next build`, started the actual production server, curled real HTTP output, and rendered every new surface with Playwright (Chromium at `/opt/pw-browsers/chromium-1194`, `NODE_PATH=/opt/node22/lib/node_modules`). Found two real, render/curl-verified bugs — not source-read guesses.

**`error.tsx` — PASS.** Correct `"use client"` client component, correct `{ error: Error & { digest?: string }, reset: () => void }` typing. Verified the "never leaks error.message" claim empirically, not just by reading the source: added a temporary `force-dynamic` throwing test route (`src/app/qa-throw-test/page.tsx`, deleted before this review's commit — confirmed via `git status --short` clean afterward), hit it against the real production server. Response is a genuine HTTP 500. Rendered the page in Playwright and read `document.body.innerText` programmatically: the injected marker string (`QA-injected-test-error-secret-stack-trace-marker-12345`) does not appear anywhere in the rendered body or in any browser console message captured during the run. Screenshot confirms a clean, on-brand "Something went wrong" card with only `Try again`/`Back to home` — no stack trace, no error text, in the UI.

**`not-found.tsx` — PASS.** `curl -o /dev/null -w "%{http_code}"` against a nonexistent path returns a genuine `404` (not a 200 masquerading as a styled 404 page) — checked the actual HTTP status, not just that a nice page renders. Playwright renders confirm the branded 404 shell at 390px/1280px, dark/light, no overflow (`scrollWidth === clientWidth` checked programmatically in all 4 combinations).

**`robots.ts`/`sitemap.ts` — PASS.** Ran a real `next build && next start` and curled the actual compiled output (not read from source):
```
User-Agent: *
Allow: /
Disallow: /app/
Disallow: /api/
Sitemap: https://www.alxioum.com/sitemap.xml
```
`/sitemap.xml` returns real XML listing exactly the 7 public routes claimed (`/`, `/login`, `/signup`, `/choose-plan`, `/privacy`, `/terms`, `/faq`), all resolving to `https://www.alxioum.com/...` (the corrected, non-stale `siteUrl`). Confirmed all 7 listed URLs actually return `200` when curled directly. `/robots.txt` and `/sitemap.xml` both appear as real compiled routes in the `next build` route table.

**BUG 1 (real, verified via curl, not source-reading) — canonical tag is wrong on `/login`, `/signup`, and `/choose-plan`, contradicting the sitemap that lists them as distinct indexable pages.** Root layout sets `alternates: { canonical: "/" }` in `src/app/layout.tsx`'s metadata object. Next.js resolves a relative canonical against `metadataBase` only, not against the actual requested route, so every page that doesn't override this with its own `metadata.alternates.canonical` inherits a canonical pointing at the bare homepage. `/privacy`, `/terms`, and `/faq` each correctly set their own absolute canonical (verified: `<link rel="canonical" href="https://www.alxioum.com/privacy"/>` etc., curled directly) — but `/login`, `/signup`, and `/choose-plan` have no `metadata` export at all, so each renders `<link rel="canonical" href="https://www.alxioum.com"/>` (curled and confirmed directly, all three). This means the sitemap simultaneously tells crawlers "these are three distinct, indexable pages" (with their own `priority`/`changefreq`) while the canonical tag on each tells crawlers "actually, index the homepage instead, this page is a duplicate" — a self-contradictory, incorrect signal that undermines the very sitemap this wave shipped. Low severity (not security, not user-facing), but a real defect in exactly the surface (canonical tags) this wave's own brief asked to be verified, not just read from source. **Needs a fix**: either give `/login`, `/signup`, `/choose-plan` their own `metadata.alternates.canonical` (matching the pattern already used correctly on `/privacy`/`/terms`/`/faq`), or drop the root layout's blanket canonical default entirely since a wrong canonical on 3 of 7 sitemap-listed pages is worse than none.

**BUG 2 (real, render-verified, blocking) — the new `CookieBanner` visually and functionally covers the mobile bottom-nav bar (`BottomNav.tsx`) for every first-time visitor on mobile, including brand-new signups on their very first `/app` session.** `CookieBanner` is `fixed inset-x-0 bottom-0 z-50`, mounted unconditionally in root `layout.tsx` (a sibling to `AuthProvider`, so it renders on every page including all authenticated `/app/**` pages, not just the public marketing pages it's visually designed for) with no `md:hidden`. `BottomNav.tsx` (the app's 5-tab primary mobile navigation) is `fixed inset-x-0 bottom-0 z-40 md:hidden`. Verified this is a real collision, not a theoretical one: built a minimal test harness (`src/app/qa-nav-test/page.tsx`, rendering the actual imported `BottomNav` component — real code, not approximated classNames — under the real root layout which also mounts the real `CookieBanner`; deleted before this review's commit, `git status --short` confirmed clean) and measured real Playwright bounding boxes at 390px: `BottomNav` occupies `y:[745,844]`, `CookieBanner` occupies `y:[672,844]` — full overlap, and since `CookieBanner` has the higher z-index, it renders on top, completely obscuring the bottom nav's tab icons (screenshot confirms only the very bottom edge/labels of the nav peek out below the banner's Accept button). This means: any user's literal first action after landing in the app (whether via the public landing page or straight into `/app` post-signup) has their primary navigation blocked by the cookie banner until they tap "Accept" — on the exact device class (mobile) and exact moment (first session) this app cares most about getting right. This is precisely the class of bug the project's standing checklist warns about (a fixed/positioned UI element that reads fine in isolation but collides with a sibling once actually rendered together) — reading `CookieBanner.tsx` or `BottomNav.tsx` in isolation would not have caught this; only rendering them together did. **Needs a fix from Dev**: `CookieBanner` needs mobile-safe bottom offset/positioning so it doesn't overlap `BottomNav` on authenticated pages (e.g. account for the nav's height + safe-area on mobile, or make the banner pathname-aware and shift up under `/app/**`) — I'm flagging the bug and its cause, not prescribing the exact implementation, per my role boundary.

**Cookie banner content/logic — mostly PASS, with the positioning bug above as the one real defect.** Independently re-verified the "zero analytics/ad cookies" claim myself rather than trusting the code comment: `grep -rniE "gtag|google-analytics|fbq\(|mixpanel|amplitude|hotjar|posthog|plausible|clarity\.ms"` across `src/` and `public/` — zero hits. `grep -rn "document\.cookie"` and any `cookies().set`/`response.cookies.set` — zero hits anywhere in the app. The banner's copy is accurate. `localStorage` consent logic traced end to end: wrapped in `try/catch` on both read and write, fails silently (banner doesn't show / doesn't persist) rather than throwing in a privacy-mode/blocked-storage browser — confirmed via Playwright that a fresh session shows the banner, clicking Accept hides it immediately, and a full page reload after accepting correctly does **not** re-show it (real reload, not assumed).

**Privacy/Terms copy — read in full, mostly accurate, one real (non-blocking) overstatement found.** Cross-checked every substantive claim against real code, not just internal consistency:
- "We do not sell your data," Anthropic/Stripe/Supabase processing disclosures, "US-hosted" (Supabase), minors-as-users handling — all match what the app actually does (confirmed API routes do call Anthropic, Stripe billing routes exist, no data-sale mechanism anywhere in the codebase).
- "Self-service account deletion is being added... until it ships, email us" (both Privacy and Terms) — verified accurate: grepped for any `auth.admin.deleteUser`/account-deletion route/UI anywhere in the app — genuinely doesn't exist yet, matches Wave 3's own plan that account deletion is a separate, not-yet-built item. Not an overstatement.
- **Real, minor overstatement found**: Terms of Service says "When you sign up, you choose a track (student or founder)," implying both are currently live options — but `src/app/choose-plan/page.tsx` still gates the business/founder option with `comingSoon = track === "business"` and an "opacity-60"/"Coming soon" badge (confirmed unchanged, matches the state Cato's own triage documented earlier in this file). New signups cannot actually choose founder today. Low severity, not blocking (the `LegalDraftNotice` already flags the whole page as non-binding draft pending review), but worth a copy tweak before this is ever taken out of draft status.
- `[COMPANY NAME]`/`[CONTACT EMAIL]`/`[ADDRESS]` placeholders used consistently everywhere a real corporate/legal identity fact would be needed (grepped both files) — nothing fabricated.
- `LegalDraftNotice` genuinely visually prominent, not buried: renders as a warning-toned card directly under the `<h1>`/"draft last updated" line on both pages, confirmed via full-page Playwright screenshot at 390px/1280px, dark/light (8 renders total) — impossible to miss on either page in any combination.
- `warning`/`warning-soft` Tailwind tokens the notice depends on are real, defined in `globals.css`/`tailwind.config.ts` (confirmed, not assumed).

**OG/Twitter metadata — PASS.** `ogImage = "/icons/icon-512.png"` resolves to a real 229,616-byte PNG on disk, and `curl` against the running production server confirms `200 image/png` at that path — not just a file-exists check, an actual HTTP fetch. `og:title`/`og:description`/`twitter:title`/`twitter:description` all render with real content pulled from `branding`, curled and confirmed directly from the live homepage HTML.

**Regression/scope discipline — PASS.** `git diff 81875a8 b7944cd` touches exactly 12 files (11 code + `PROJECT_STATE.md`), zero touches to `supabase/schema.sql`, zero `.upsert()` calls introduced, zero touches to the track-lock trigger or any RLS policy, zero touches to `StudentHome.tsx`/`BusinessHome.tsx`'s grid structure. Business-track catalog imports in `BusinessOnboarding.tsx` unaffected (still correctly importing `BUSINESS_*` catalogs, re-grepped). No entitlement/billing columns anywhere near this diff.

**Build gates — run fresh and independently, not taken on Cato's word.** Symlinked `node_modules` from the sibling checkout (this worktree ships without its own). `rm -rf .next && npx tsc --noEmit`: clean, exit 0. `npx next build`: clean, exit 0, 48 real routes including `/robots.txt` and `/sitemap.xml` as genuine compiled routes (confirmed by curling the running `next start` server, not just reading the build's route table).

**Verdict: SIGN-OFF BLOCKED.** Two real, independently render/curl-verified bugs, not source-read guesses:
1. **Blocking**: `CookieBanner` (`src/components/shared/CookieBanner.tsx`) covers/blocks the mobile `BottomNav` (`src/components/shared/BottomNav.tsx`) for every first-time mobile visitor, including brand-new signups on their first `/app` session — verified via real bounding boxes on the actual components rendered together, full overlap of the nav's icon row. This is a functional navigation regression on the app's highest-traffic mobile surface at the worst possible moment (first session), not a cosmetic nitpick.
2. **Non-blocking on its own, but should be fixed in the same pass**: `alternates.canonical` is wrong (points at the bare homepage) on `/login`, `/signup`, `/choose-plan` — contradicts the sitemap this same wave shipped.
3. **Non-blocking, flag for before "draft" status is ever lifted**: Terms of Service overstates founder-track availability given the current `comingSoon` gate on `/choose-plan`.

Everything else in this wave (`error.tsx`, `not-found.tsx`, `robots.ts`/`sitemap.ts`'s disallow rules and content, the "zero analytics cookies" claim, `localStorage` consent persistence, OG image resolution, `LegalDraftNotice` prominence, Privacy Policy's substantive claims, build gates, regression/scope discipline) is independently verified clean and does not need rework.
